{\cal T}_{u,D}(\ell):=(W\ell_{u}\eta_{u})^{-2}\exp\left(-\left(\ell/\ell_{u}\right)_{+}^{1/2}\right)+W^{-D}.

Both
{\cal T}^{(\cal L-K)}_{u}
and
{\cal T}_{u,D}
are non-decreasing funtions:

0\leq\ell_{1}\leq\ell_{2},\quad\quad{\cal T}^{(\cal L-K)}_{u}(\ell_{1})\geq{\cal T}^{(\cal L-K)}_{u}(\ell_{2}),\quad{\cal T}_{u,D}(\ell_{1})\geq{\cal T}_{u,D}(\ell_{2})

Denote the ratio between them by

(5.28)

{\cal J}_{u,D}(\ell):=\left({\cal T}^{(\cal L-K)}_{u}(\ell)\Big/{\cal T}_{u,D}(\ell)\right)+1

We aim to bound
{\cal J}_{u,D}(\ell)
for any
u\in[s,t]
and large
D>0
, i.e.,

(5.29)

\displaystyle{\cal J}^{*}_{u,D}:=\max_{\ell}{\cal J}_{u,D}(\ell)\leq(\eta_{s}/\eta_{u})^{4}

Define a scale parameter

\ell^{*}_{t}:=(\log W)^{3/2}\cdot\ell_{t}.

In this scale
\ell_{t}^{*}
,
\Theta_{t}
is exponentially small, whereas
{\cal T}_{t}
is not.
More precisely, we have the following lemma.

Lemma 5.6
.

For any fixed large
D
and small
\delta>0
,

(5.30)

\displaystyle|b-a|\geq\delta\cdot\ell_{t}^{*}

\displaystyle\implies\left({\Theta}_{t}\right)_{ab}\leq W^{-D},\quad\left({\Theta}_{s}^{-1}{\Theta}_{t}\right)_{ab}\leq W^{-D}

(5.31)

\displaystyle|b-a|\geq\delta\cdot\ell_{t}^{*}

\displaystyle\implies{\cal L}_{t,(-,+),(a,b)}\prec{\cal J}^{*}_{t,D}\cdot{\cal T}_{t,D}(|a-b|).

For any constant
C>0
,

(5.32)

{{\cal T}_{u,D}\left(\ell-C\cdot\ell_{u}^{*}\right)}\;\prec\;{\cal T}_{u,D}\left(\ell\right).

Proof.

We will only prove (
5.31
). By definition,

{\cal L}_{t,(-,+),(a,b)}=\left({\cal L}-\cal K\right)_{t,(-,+),(a,b)}+{\cal K}_{t,(-,+),(a,b)}.

Using the
\Theta
representation of the 2-
\cal K
in (
2.57
) and the decay property of
\Theta
from (
2.52
), we have for any
D>0
that

{\cal K}_{t,(-,+),(a,b)}\prec N^{-D}\leq{\cal T}_{t,D}(|a-b|).

Together with the definition (
5.29
) of
\cal J^{*}
, (
5.28
), and (
5.26
), we have proved (
5.31
).
We remark that (
5.31
) is significant in that
{\cal T}_{t,D}

is of
\left(W\eta_{u}\ell_{u}\right)^{-1}
smaller than the typical size of
2-{\cal L}
loop. The reason we gain an extra small factor is due to the assumption
|b-a|\geq\delta\cdot\ell_{t}^{*}
.
∎

Our proof of (
2.76
) relies on the loop hierarchy (
5.3
) in the special case
n=2
.
We begin the analysis of the hierarchy by bounding terms in (
5.3
).

Lemma 5.7
.

Suppose the assumptions of Theorem
2.21
and the conclusions of Step 1, i.e., (
2.73
) and (
2.74
),
hold. Assume that

(5.33)

\displaystyle s\leq u\leq t,\quad D\geq 10,\;\textbf{a}=(a_{1},a_{2}),\;\textbf{a}^{\prime}=(a^{\prime}_{1},a^{\prime}_{2}),\;\boldsymbol{\sigma}=(+,-),\quad\max_{i}|a_{i}-a_{i}^{\prime}|\leq\ell_{t}^{*}.

We have

(5.34)

\displaystyle{\cal E}^{((L-K)\times(L-K))}_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\Big/\;{\cal T}_{t,D}(|a_{1}-a_{2}|)\quad\;

\displaystyle\;\prec\;\left(\eta_{u}\right)^{-1}\cdot\left(W\eta_{u}\ell_{u}\right)^{-1}\cdot\left({\cal J}^{*}_{u,D}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{(\widetilde{G})}_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\;\Big/\;{\cal T}_{t,D}(|a_{1}-a_{2}|)\;

\displaystyle\;\prec\;\left(\eta_{u}\right)^{-1}\cdot\left(\ell_{u}/\ell_{s}\right)^{2}\cdot{\bf 1}(|a_{1}-a_{2}|\leq\ell_{u}^{*})

(5.35)

\displaystyle\;+\;\left(\eta_{u}\right)^{-1}\cdot\left(W\eta_{u}\ell_{u}\right)^{-1/3}\cdot\left({\cal J}^{*}_{u,D}\right)^{3}

\displaystyle\left(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{E}\right)_{u,\,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\,{\textbf{a}},\,{\textbf{a}}^{\prime}}\;\Big/\;\left({\cal T}_{t,D}(|a_{1}-a_{2}|)\right)^{2}\;

\displaystyle\;\prec\;\left(\eta_{u}\right)^{-1}\cdot\left(\ell_{u}/\ell_{s}\right)^{5}\cdot{\bf 1}(|a_{1}-a_{2}|\leq 4\ell_{t}^{*})

(5.36)

\displaystyle\;+\;\left(\eta_{t}\right)^{-1}\cdot\left(W\eta_{u}\ell_{u}\right)^{-1/2}\cdot\left({\cal J}^{*}_{u,D}\right)^{3}

By assumption (
2.72
),
s
and
t
are near each other. So the exact exponents on the right side of (
5.34
)-(
5.7
) are not important for our purpose. We only need the errors are of the form

(\eta_{s}/\eta_{t})^{\alpha}\cdot\left(W\eta_{u}\ell_{u}\right)^{-\beta}\cdot\left({\cal J}^{*}_{u,D}\right)^{\gamma}

for some positive constants
\alpha
,
\beta
and
\gamma
.

We now provide a power counting to guess the sizes of terms in the previous lemma. Since

{\cal E}^{((L-K)\times(L-K))}
is a higher order term, we will ignore it in the following heuristic.
Denote by
A_{u}\sim W\ell_{u}\eta_{u}
. By definition,
\mathcal{E}^{(\widetilde{G})}_{u}
is a product of an
1
-
\cal L-\cal K
loop and
a
3
-
\cal L
loop. We know that
1
-
\cal L-\cal K
loop is of order
A_{u}^{-1}
and
3
-
\cal L
loop is of order
A_{u}^{-2}
. In addition,
the summation index in
\mathcal{E}^{(\widetilde{G})}
yields a factor
\ell_{u}
if we assume the correct decay property. Since there is an additional
W
factor in
\mathcal{E}^{(\widetilde{G})}_{u}
,

(5.37)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{(\widetilde{G})}_{u}\sim A_{u}^{-3}W\ell_{u}=\eta_{u}^{-1}A_{u}^{-2}.

The factor
A_{u}^{-2}
is exactly the prefactor in the definition of
{\cal T}_{u}
in (
5.35
). For
\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{E}_{u}
, it is a
6
-
\cal L
loop of order
A_{u}^{-5}
. Hence

(5.38)

\displaystyle(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{E})_{u}\sim A_{u}^{-5}W\ell_{u}=\eta_{u}^{-1}A_{u}^{-4},

which explains the order in (
5.7
). Notice that in both (
5.35
) and (
5.7
),
we used
{\cal T}_{t}
on the left sides of the equations while both
\mathcal{E}^{(\widetilde{G})}_{u}
and
(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{E})_{u}
are at the time
u
.

So far we only used the loop bounds which are consequences of Step 1.
It remains to understand the last terms in (
5.35
) and (
5.7
). Assuming Lemma
5.7
, we now prove (
2.76
). We will use extensively the kernel estimates concerning the operator
\cal U
in Section
7.1
.

Proof of (
2.76
).

By assumption (
2.69
) on
({\cal L-\cal K})_{s}
, the operator norm bound on
\cal U
in Lemma
7.1
, and the tail estimate (
7.3
),
we can bound the first term on the right side of (
5.3
) by

(5.39)

\left(\mathcal{U}_{s,t,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\circ(\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K})_{s,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\right)_{\textbf{a}}\;\Big/\;{\cal T}_{t,D}(|a_{1}-a_{2}|)\quad\prec\;(\ell_{t}/\ell_{s})^{2}\cdot{\bf 1}(|a_{1}-a_{2}|\leq\ell_{t}^{*})+1,

where the last term of order one comes from applying (
7.3
). Notice that the expansion factor
\left(\eta_{u}/\eta_{t}\right)^{2}
from applying Lemma
7.1
has become

(\ell_{t}/\ell_{s})^{2}
due to the prefactors in
{\cal T}_{s,D}
and
{\cal T}_{t,D}
.
For any
a
fixed and any function
f
, we decompose
f=f_{1}+f_{2}
where

f_{1}(\textbf{b})=f(\textbf{b}){\bf 1}(\|\textbf{b}-\textbf{a}\|\leq\ell_{t}^{*})
.
From the decay of
{\cal U}_{u,t}
,
\Big({\cal U}_{u,t,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\circ f_{2}\Big)_{\textbf{a}}
is exponentially small.
Hence we only have to bound
{\cal U}_{u,t,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\circ f_{1}
, for which we apply Lemma
7.1
. Therefore,
we can bound the second term of (
5.3
) by

\Big({\cal U}_{u,t,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\circ{\cal E}^{((L-K)\times(L-K))}_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\Big)_{\textbf{a}}\prec(\eta_{u}/\eta_{t})^{2}\max_{\|\textbf{b}-\textbf{a}\|\leq\ell_{t}^{*}}{\cal E}^{((L-K)\times(L-K))}_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{b}}+W^{-D},

\|\textbf{b}-\textbf{a}\|=\max_{i}|b_{i}-a_{i}|\leq\ell_{t}^{*}.

Under the last condition, (
5.32
) implies that

{\cal T}_{t,D}(|b_{1}-b_{2}|)\prec{\cal T}_{t,D}(|a_{1}-a_{2}|).

Using
{\cal E}^{((L-K)\times(L-K))}
estimate (
5.34
) and
(s,t)
-condition (
2.72
), we have

(5.40)

\displaystyle\Big({\cal U}_{u,t,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\circ{\cal E}^{((L-K)\times(L-K))}_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\Big)_{\textbf{a}}\;\Big/\;{\cal T}_{t,D}(|a_{1}-a_{2}|)\quad

\displaystyle\prec\;\frac{1}{\eta_{u}}\left(\eta_{u}/\eta_{t}\right)^{2}\cdot\left(W\eta_{u}\ell_{u}\right)^{-1}\cdot\left({\cal J}^{*}_{u,D}\right)^{2}

Similarly, the estimate (
5.35
) on
{\cal E}^{(\widetilde{G})}
implies

\displaystyle\Big(\mathcal{U}_{u,t,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\circ\mathcal{E}^{(\widetilde{G})}_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\Big)_{\mathbf{a}}\;\Big/\;{\cal T}_{t,D}(|a_{1}-a_{2}|)

\displaystyle\prec\;{\bf 1}(|a_{1}-a_{2}|\leq 3\ell_{t}^{*})\cdot\frac{1}{\eta_{u}}\cdot\left(\eta_{u}/\eta_{t}\right)^{2}\cdot\left(\ell_{u}/\ell_{s}\right)^{2}

(5.41)

\displaystyle+\;\frac{1}{\eta_{u}}\cdot\left(\eta_{u}/\eta_{t}\right)^{2}\cdot\left(W\eta_{u}\ell_{u}\right)^{-1/3}\cdot\left({\cal J}^{*}_{u,D}\right)^{3},

and the estimate (
5.7
) on
\cal E\otimes\cal E
implies

\displaystyle\left(\left(\mathcal{U}_{u,t,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\otimes\mathcal{U}_{u,t,\overline{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}\right)\;\circ\;\left(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{E}\right)_{u,\,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\right)_{{\textbf{a}},{\textbf{a}}}\;\Big/\;\left({\cal T}_{t,D}(|a_{1}-a_{2}|)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\prec\;{\bf 1}(|a_{1}-a_{2}|\leq 6\ell_{t}^{*})\cdot\frac{1}{\eta_{u}}\cdot\left(\eta_{u}/\eta_{t}\right)^{4}\cdot\left(\ell_{u}/\ell_{s}\right)^{5}

(5.42)

\displaystyle+\;\frac{1}{\eta_{u}}\cdot\left(W\eta_{u}\ell_{u}\right)^{-1/3}\cdot\left({\cal J}^{*}_{u,D}\right)^{3}

In the last inequality, we have absorbed the expansion factor
\left(\eta_{u}/\eta_{t}\right)^{4}
by the change of the exponent in
\left(W\eta_{u}\ell_{u}\right)
from
-1/2
to
-1/3
.

We now insert these bounds into the
2
-
G
-loop equation (
5.3
) and
bound the martingale term by (
5.24
).
Denote by
T
the stopping time

(5.43)

\displaystyle T:=\min\{u:{\cal J}^{*}_{u,D}\geq\left(\eta_{s}/\eta_{t}\right)^{4}\}

and set
\tau=T\wedge t
.
Clearly, the stopped versions of Lemma
5.7
and the previous bounds in this proof are valid by similar arguments.
The quadratic variation of the stopped martingale term is then bounded by

\displaystyle\int_{s}^{\tau}\left(\left(\mathcal{U}_{u,t,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\otimes\mathcal{U}_{u,t,\overline{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}\right)\;\circ\;\left(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{E}\right)_{u,\,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\,\overline{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}\right)_{{\textbf{a}},{\textbf{a}}}du\Big/{\cal T}_{t,D}(|a_{1}-a_{2}|)^{2}

\displaystyle\;\prec\;

\displaystyle\int_{s}^{\tau}du\Big\{{\bf 1}(|a_{1}-a_{2}|\leq 6\ell_{t}^{*})\cdot\frac{1}{\eta_{u}}\cdot\left(\eta_{u}/\eta_{t}\right)^{4}\cdot\left(\ell_{u}/\ell_{s}\right)^{5}+\;\frac{1}{\eta_{u}}\cdot\left(W\eta_{u}\ell_{u}\right)^{-1/3}\cdot\left({\cal J}^{*}_{u,D}\right)^{3}\Big\}

(5.44)

\displaystyle\;\leq\;

\displaystyle\big[(\eta_{s}/\eta_{t})^{4}\cdot{\bf 1}(|a_{1}-a_{2}|\leq 6\ell_{t}^{*})+1\big].

Applying (
5.24
), we have

\int_{s}^{\tau}\left(\mathcal{U}_{u,t,\sigma}\circ\mathcal{E}_{u,\sigma}^{(M)}\right)_{\textbf{a}}\;\Big/{\cal T}_{t,D}(|a_{1}-a_{2}|)\prec\big[(\eta_{s}/\eta_{t})^{2}\cdot{\bf 1}(|a_{1}-a_{2}|\leq 6\ell_{t}^{*})+1\big].

This statement holds for any
t\geq s
. Furthermore, due to the monotonicity of
\ell
and
\eta
, for any
s\leq t^{\prime}\leq t
we have

(5.45)

\displaystyle\int_{s}^{T\wedge t^{\prime}}\left(\mathcal{U}_{u,t^{\prime},\sigma}\circ\mathcal{E}_{u,\sigma}^{(M)}\right)_{\textbf{a}}\;\Big/{\cal T}_{t^{\prime},D}(|a_{1}-a_{2}|)

\displaystyle\;\prec\;\big[(\eta_{s}/\eta_{t^{\prime}})^{2}\cdot{\bf 1}(|a_{1}-a_{2}|\leq 6\ell_{t^{\prime}}^{*})+1\big]

\displaystyle\;\prec\;\big[(\eta_{s}/\eta_{t})^{2}\cdot{\bf 1}(|a_{1}-a_{2}|\leq 6\ell_{t}^{*})+1\big]

We extend this bound to a
N^{-C}
net between
s
and
t
, i.e.,
t^{(k)}=s+(t-s)\cdot k\cdot N^{-C}
for a fixed large
C
.

(5.46)

\displaystyle\max_{k}\int_{s}^{T\wedge t^{(k)}}\left(\mathcal{U}_{u,\,t^{(k)},\,\sigma}\circ\mathcal{E}_{u,\sigma}^{(M)}\right)_{\textbf{a}}\;\Big/{\cal T}_{t^{(k)},\,D}(|a_{1}-a_{2}|)\prec\big[(\eta_{s}/\eta_{t})^{2}\cdot{\bf 1}(|a_{1}-a_{2}|\leq 6\ell_{t}^{*})+1\big]

For any
k
, we can rewrite the last term in (
5.3
) as follows:

{\cal U}_{t,\tau}\circ\int_{s}^{\tau}\left(\mathcal{U}_{u,t,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\circ\mathcal{E}^{(M)}_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\right)_{\textbf{a}}\;=\;{\cal U}_{t^{(k)},\tau}\int_{s}^{\tau}\left(\mathcal{U}_{u,\,t^{(k)},\,\sigma}\circ\mathcal{E}_{u,\sigma}^{(M)}\right)_{\textbf{a}}

Choose a
k
such that
t^{(k-1)}\leq\tau<t^{(k)}
. Then
T\wedge t^{(k)}=\tau
.
Since
C
can be arbitrary fixed large,
{\cal U}_{t^{(k)},\tau}
is nearly an identity operator. Then

{\cal U}_{t^{(k)},\tau}\int_{s}^{\tau}\left(\mathcal{U}_{u,\,t^{(k)},\,\sigma}\circ\mathcal{E}_{u,\sigma}^{(M)}\right)_{\textbf{a}}=\int_{s}^{\tau}\left(\mathcal{U}_{u,\,t^{(k)},\,\sigma}\circ\mathcal{E}_{u,\sigma}^{(M)}\right)_{\textbf{a}}+O(N^{-C/2})

and

{\cal T}_{t^{(k)},\,D}(|a_{1}-a_{2}|)\Big/{\cal T}_{\tau,\,D}(|a_{1}-a_{2}|)=1+O(N^{-C/2})

Combining these two bounds, we have

{\cal U}_{t,\tau}\circ\int_{s}^{\tau}\left(\mathcal{U}_{u,t,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\circ\mathcal{E}^{(M)}_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\right)_{\textbf{a}}\;\Big/\;{\cal T}_{\tau,\,D}(|a_{1}-a_{2}|)\prec\big[(\eta_{s}/\eta_{t})^{2}\cdot{\bf 1}(|a_{1}-a_{2}|\leq 6\ell_{t}^{*})+1\big]

Combining this bound with (
5.39
), (
5.40
), (
5.3
) and (
5.3
), we have

\left({\cal L}-{\cal K}\right)_{\tau,\textbf{a}}\Big/{\cal T}_{t,D}(|a_{1}-a_{2}|)\prec\big[(\eta_{s}/\eta_{t})^{2}\cdot{\bf 1}(|a_{1}-a_{2}|\leq 6\ell_{t}^{*})+1\big],

where we have used the initial condition
{\cal J}^{*}_{s,D}\prec 1
from (
2.68
) and (
2.69
).

This implies that

(5.47)

\displaystyle{\cal J}^{*}_{\tau,D}\prec(\eta_{s}/\eta_{t})^{2}

Hence
{\mathbb{P}}(T\leq t)
is negligible and we have completed the proof of (
2.76
).
Notice that we have also proved

(5.48)

\displaystyle\left({\cal L}-{\cal K}\right)_{t,\textbf{a}}\Big/{\cal T}_{t,D}(|a_{1}-a_{2}|)\prec\big[(\eta_{s}/\eta_{t})^{2}\cdot{\bf 1}(|a_{1}-a_{2}|\leq 6\ell_{t}^{*})+1\big].

∎

Proof of Lemma
5.7
.

Proof of (
5.34
).

We first note the monotonicity properties

u\leq t\implies\ell_{u}\leq\ell_{t},\quad{\cal T}_{u,D}\leq{\cal T}_{t,D}.

By definition,

(5.49)

\displaystyle{\cal E}^{(({\cal L-K})\times({\cal L-K}))}_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}=

\displaystyle W\sum_{b_{1},b_{2}}({\cal L-K})_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma},(a_{1},b_{1})}S^{(B)}_{b_{1},b_{2}}({\cal L-K})_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma},(b_{2},a_{2})}

By definition of
S^{(B)}
, we have
|b_{1}-b_{2}|\leq 1
.
Using

\int_{0}^{a}\exp\left(-\sqrt{(a-x)}-\sqrt{x}+\sqrt{a}\right)dx\leq C\approx 6.12,\quad\text{ and }\quad\int_{0}^{\infty}\exp\left({-\sqrt{x}}\right)dx=2

we have

\displaystyle{\cal E}^{(({\cal L-K})\times({\cal L-K}))}_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\;\prec\;

\displaystyle W({\cal J}_{u,D}^{*})^{2}\cdot\sum_{x}{\cal T}_{u,\,D}(|a_{1}-x|)\cdot{\cal T}_{u,\,D}(|a_{2}-x|)

(5.50)

\displaystyle\;\prec\;

\displaystyle({\cal J}_{u,D}^{*})^{2}\cdot\eta_{u}^{-1}\cdot(W\ell_{u}\eta_{u})^{-1}\cdot{\cal T}_{u,D}(|a_{1}-a_{2}|).

We have thus proved (
5.34
).

Proof of (
5.35
)
. By definition, we write

(5.51)

\displaystyle\max_{\textbf{a}}{\cal E}^{(\widetilde{G})}_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\prec W\sum_{b_{1},b_{2}}\langle\widetilde{G}_{u}E_{b_{1}}\rangle\cdot S^{(B)}_{b_{1},b_{2}}\cdot{\cal L}_{u,(-,+,+),(a_{1},b_{2},a_{2})}+c.c.,\quad\widetilde{G}=G-m

By (
4.5
) and (
2.74
), we have

\langle\widetilde{G}_{u}E_{b_{1}}\rangle\prec\Xi^{(\cal L)}_{u,2}\cdot(W\ell_{u}\eta_{u})^{-1}\prec(W\ell_{u}\eta_{u})^{-1}+{\cal J}^{*}_{u,D}\cdot(W\ell_{u}\eta_{u})^{-2}.

Therefore

(5.52)

\displaystyle\max_{\textbf{a}}{\cal E}^{(\widetilde{G})}_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\prec\frac{1}{\ell_{u}\eta_{u}}\cdot\sum_{b}\left|{\cal L}_{u,(-,+,+),(a_{1},b,a_{2})}\right|(1+{\cal J}^{*}_{u,D}(W\ell_{u}\eta_{u})^{-1})

Consider first the case
|a_{1}-a_{2}|\leq\ell_{u}^{*}
. Denote

\ell_{u}^{**}:=\ell_{u}\cdot(\log W)^{3}=\ell^{*}_{u}\cdot(\log W)^{3/2}

Clearly,
{\cal T}_{u,D}(\ell_{u}^{**})\prec W^{-D}
.
For
|b-a_{1}|\leq\ell_{u}^{**}
,
the loop bound (
2.73
) proved in the Step 1 implies that

(5.53)

\displaystyle\sum_{b}{\bf 1}\big(|b-a_{1}|\leq\ell_{u}^{**}\big)\cdot\left|{\cal L}_{u,(-,+,+),(a_{1},b,a_{2})}\right|\prec(\ell_{u}/\ell_{s})^{2}\cdot(W\ell_{u}\eta_{u})^{-2}\cdot\ell_{u}

where the summation over
b
provides a factor
\ell_{u}^{**}\prec\ell_{u}
.
If
|b-a_{1}|\geq\ell_{u}^{**}
, we can easily bound

\left|{\cal L}_{u,(-,+,+),(a_{1},b,a_{2})}\right|\prec\max_{x_{1}\in{\cal I}_{a_{1}},y\in{\cal I}_{b}}|G_{x_{1},y}|.

Using (
4.2
), we have that

\max_{x_{1}\in{\cal I}_{a_{1}},y\in{\cal I}_{b}}|G_{x_{1},y}|\prec\sum_{a_{1}^{\prime},b^{\prime}}\left({\cal L}_{u,(+,-),{(a_{1}^{\prime},b^{\prime})}}\right)^{1/2}{\bf 1}\left(|a^{\prime}_{1}-a_{1}|\leq 1,|b-b^{\prime}|\leq 1\right).

Since
|b-a_{1}|\geq\ell_{u}^{**}
, by (
5.31
) and
{\cal T}_{u,D}(|a_{1}-b|)\prec W^{-D}
, we obtain that

{\cal L}_{u,(+,-),{(a_{1}^{\prime},b^{\prime})}}\prec{\cal J}^{*}_{u,D}\cdot W^{-D}.

Therefore, the contribution from
|b-a_{1}|\geq\ell_{u}^{**}
part is negligible in the sense

(5.54)

\displaystyle\sum_{b}{\bf 1}\big(|b-a_{1}|\geq\ell_{u}^{**}\big)\cdot\left|{\cal L}_{u,(-,+,+),(a_{1},b,a_{2})}\right|\prec{\cal J}^{*}_{u,D}\cdot W^{-10}.

It is easy to check that the contribution of the last term does not affect the argument given below. Furthermore, the contributions from both (
5.53
) and (
5.52
) are bounded by

(5.55)

\displaystyle|a_{1}-a_{2}|\leq\ell_{u}^{*}\implies\mathcal{E}_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\mathbf{a}}^{(\widetilde{G})}\Big/{\cal T}_{u,D}(|a_{1}-a_{2}|)\prec(\eta_{u})^{-1}\cdot\left(\ell_{u}/\ell_{s}\right)^{2}\cdot\left(1+(\mathcal{J}_{u,D}^{*})^{2}\left(W\ell_{u}\eta_{u}\right)^{-1}\right).

This implies that (
5.35
) holds for
|a_{1}-a_{2}|\leq\ell_{u}^{*}
.

For
|a_{1}-a_{2}|\geq\ell_{u}^{*}
, we split it into two cases

(1):\min_{i}|a_{i}-b|\leq\ell_{u}^{*}/2,\quad\quad(2):\min_{i}|a_{i}-b|\geq\ell_{u}^{*}/2.

In the first case, we assume without loss of generality that
|a_{1}-b|\leq\ell_{u}^{*}/2
. Applying the Schwarz inequality to the two
G
edges in
{\cal L}_{u,(-,+,+),(a_{1},b,a_{2})}
connecting the block
a_{2}
, we have

G_{x_{1}x_{2}}G_{x_{2}y}G_{yx_{1}}\prec|G_{x_{1}x_{2}}|^{2}|G_{yx_{1}}|+|G_{x_{2}y}|^{2}|G_{yx_{1}}|.

Therefore, we have

(5.56)

\displaystyle{\cal L}_{u,(-,+,+),(a_{1},b,a_{2})}\;\prec\;

\displaystyle\;W^{-1}\Big({\cal L}_{u,(-,+),(a_{1},a_{2})}\ \max_{x_{1}\in{\cal I}_{a_{1}}}\sum_{y\in{\cal I}_{b}}|G_{x_{1}y}|+{\cal L}_{u,(-,+),(a_{2},b)}\ \max_{y\in{\cal I}_{b}}\sum_{x_{1}\in{\cal I}_{a_{1}}}|G_{x_{1}y}|\Big).

Using (
4.2
), the
\cal K
bound and the trivial fact
\sqrt{1+c}\leq 1+c
, we can bound
|G_{x_{1}x_{2}}|
by

{\bf 1}(x_{1}\neq x_{2})|G_{x_{1}x_{2}}|\prec(W\ell_{u}\eta_{u})^{-1/2}(1+{\cal J}^{*}_{u,D}(W\ell_{u}\eta_{u})^{-1}).

It implies that for any
a
and
b
,

(5.57)

\displaystyle W^{-1}\max_{y\in{\cal I}_{b}}\sum_{x\in{\cal I}_{a}}|G_{xy}|\prec(W\ell_{u}\eta_{u})^{-1/2}(1+{\cal J}^{*}_{u,D}(W\ell_{u}\eta_{u})^{-1}).

Inserting this bound into (
5.56
), we have

(5.58)

\displaystyle{\cal L}_{u,(-,+,+),(a_{1},b,a_{2})}\;\prec\;

\displaystyle\;\left({\cal L}_{u,(-,+),(a_{1},a_{2})}+{\cal L}_{u,(-,+),(a_{2},b)}\right)\cdot(W\ell_{u}\eta_{u})^{-1/2}(1+{\cal J}^{*}_{u,D}(W\ell_{u}\eta_{u})^{-1})

Since
|a_{2}-b|\geq\ell_{u}^{*}/2
and
|a_{1}-a_{2}|\geq\ell_{u}^{*}
, we can estimate
{\cal L}_{u,(-,+),(a_{1},a_{2})}+{\cal L}_{u,(-,+),(a_{2},b)}

with (
5.31
) to have

{\cal L}_{u,(-,+,+),(a_{1},b,a_{2})}\;\prec\;{\cal J}^{*}_{u,D}\cdot\left({\cal T}_{u,D}(|a_{1}-a_{2}|)+{\cal T}_{u,D}(|b-a_{2}|)\right)\cdot(W\ell_{u}\eta_{u})^{-1/2}(1+{\cal J}^{*}_{u,D}(W\ell_{u}\eta_{u})^{-1}).

Furthermore since
|b-a_{2}|\geq|a_{1}-a_{2}|-|a_{1}-b|\geq|a_{1}-a_{2}|-\ell_{u}^{*}/2
, then

{\cal T}_{u,D}(|b-a_{2}|)\prec{\cal T}_{u,D}(|a_{1}-a_{2}|).

Combining these bounds, we obtain, for
|a_{1}-a_{2}|\geq\ell_{u}^{*}
, that

(5.59)

\displaystyle\sum_{b}^{\text{Case }1}\left|{\cal L}_{u,(-,+,+),(a_{1},b,a_{2})}\right|\prec\left({\cal J}^{*}_{u,D}\right)^{2}\cdot{\cal T}_{u,D}(|a_{1}-a_{2}|)\cdot\ell_{u}(W\ell_{u}\eta_{u})^{-1/2}.

For case (2), we bound
{\cal L}_{u,(-,+,+),(a_{1},b,a_{2})}
as follows

(5.60)

\displaystyle{\cal L}_{u,(-,+,+),(a_{1},b,a_{2})}\prec\max_{x_{1},x_{2},y}\left|G_{x_{1}y}\right|\left|G_{yx_{2}}\right|\left|G_{x_{1}x_{2}}\right|{\bf 1}(x_{1}\in{\cal I}_{a_{1}}){\bf 1}(x_{2}\in{\cal I}_{a_{2}}){\bf 1}(y\in{\cal I}_{b}).

In this case, since
a_{1}
,
a_{2}
and
b
are all different,
x_{1}
,
x_{2}
and
y
must be different.

Next we use (
4.2
) to bound a single
G
with the
2
-
G
-loops. Because
a_{1}
,
a_{2}
and
b
are away from each other by
\ell_{u}^{*}/2
, we have, by (
5.31
),

(5.61)

\displaystyle\left|G_{yx_{2}}\right|\cdot\left|G_{x_{1}x_{2}}\right|\cdot\left|G_{x_{1}y}\right|\;\prec\;

\displaystyle{\cal J}_{*}^{3/2}\Big({\cal T}_{u,D}(|a_{1}-a_{2}|){\cal T}_{u,D}(|a_{1}-b|){\cal T}_{u,D}(|a_{2}-b|)\Big)^{1/2}

Then we can bound

\displaystyle\sum_{b}^{\text{Case }2}{\cal L}_{u,(-,+,+),(a_{1},b,a_{2})}\;\prec\;

\displaystyle\;{\cal J}_{*}^{3/2}\Big({\cal T}_{u,D}(|a_{1}-a_{2}|)\Big)^{1/2}\sum_{b}\Big({\cal T}_{u,D}(|a_{1}-b|){\cal T}_{u,D}(|a_{2}-b|)\Big)^{1/2}

(5.62)

\displaystyle\;\prec\;

\displaystyle\;{\cal J}_{*}^{3/2}\Big({\cal T}_{u,D}(|a_{1}-a_{2}|)\Big)\cdot\ell_{u}\cdot(W\ell_{u}\eta_{u})^{-1}

where we have used

\int_{0}^{a}\exp\left(-\sqrt{(a-x)}/2-\sqrt{x}/2+\sqrt{a}/2\right)dx\leq C.

Combining this estimate with (
5.59
), we obtain that if
|a_{1}-a_{2}|\geq\ell_{u}^{*}
then

(5.63)

\displaystyle\sum_{b}{\cal L}_{u,(-,+,+),(a_{1},b,a_{2})}\;\prec\;

\displaystyle\;{\cal J}_{*}^{2}\Big({\cal T}_{u,D}(|a_{1}-a_{2}|)\Big)\cdot\ell_{u}\cdot(W\ell_{u}\eta_{u})^{-1/2}

Inserting it into (
5.52
) and using (
5.55
), we have completed the proof of (
5.35
).

Proof of (
5.7
).
By definition (
5.4
),
(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{E})
is the sum of
(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{E})^{(k)}
, and the latter ones can be written in terms of the following loops
{\cal L}^{(k)}:={\cal L}_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{(k)},\textbf{a}^{(k)}}
. Here

\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{(1)}=(+,-,+,-,+,-)
,
\textbf{a}^{(1)}=(a_{1},a_{2},b^{\prime},a_{2}^{\prime},a_{1}^{\prime},b)

and
\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{(2)}=

(-,+,-,+,-,+)
,
\textbf{a}^{(2)}=(a_{2},a_{1},b^{\prime},a_{1}^{\prime},a_{2}^{\prime},b)
. Hence by symmetry, we only need to prove (
5.7
) for
(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{E})^{(1)}
, i.e.,
k=1
case. Notice that
|b-b^{\prime}|=1
in this case and
we can treat
b=b^{\prime}
for all practical purpose in the following proof.

\bullet
a_{1}
\bullet
a_{2}
\bullet
b
\bullet
b^{\prime}
\bullet
a_{1}^{\prime}
\bullet
a_{2}^{\prime}
+
+
-
-
-
+
S^{(B)}
\bullet
a_{2}
\bullet
a_{1}
\bullet
b
\bullet
b^{\prime}
\bullet
a_{2}^{\prime}
\bullet
a_{1}^{\prime}
-
-
+
+
+
-
S^{(B)}

Figure 14:
Left one:
k=1
Right one:
k=2

Case 1:
|a_{1}-a_{2}|\leq 4\ell_{t}^{*}

We split the sum
\sum_{b,b^{\prime}}
into two parts

|b-a_{1}|\leq\ell_{u}^{**}:=(\log W)^{3}\ell_{u},\quad|b-a_{1}|\geq\ell_{u}^{**}

Using
{\cal T}_{u,D}(\ell_{u}^{**})
is very small, one can easily bound

\sum_{b,b^{\prime}}{\bf 1}\left(|b-a_{1}|\geq\ell_{u}^{**}\right){\cal L}^{(1)}\prec{\cal J}^{*}_{u,D}\cdot W^{-3},

by arguments similar to those used in (
5.54
).
For
|b-a_{1}|\leq\ell_{u}^{**}
, we use
the loop bound (
2.73
) proved in Step 1 to have

(5.64)

\displaystyle\sum_{b,b^{\prime}}{\bf 1}\big(|b-a_{1}|\leq\ell_{u}^{**}\big){\cal L}^{(1)}\prec(\ell_{u}/\ell_{s})^{5}\cdot(W\ell_{u}\eta_{u})^{-5}\cdot\ell_{u}.

Combining these two bounds, we obtain that (
5.7
) for
|a_{1}-a_{2}|\leq 4\ell_{t}^{*}
. Notice that the application of the loop bound of length
6
yields a very strong bound
(W\ell_{u}\eta_{u})^{-5}
which is not easy to see without the loop estimate.

Case 2:
|a_{1}-a_{2}|\geq 4\ell_{t}^{*}
.

Recall the assumption
|a^{\prime}_{i}-a_{i}|\leq\ell_{t}^{*}
(
5.33
) and the fact that we can treat
b=b^{\prime}
in the following proof.
We split the sum over
b
(and
b^{\prime}
) into two parts

(1):|b-a_{1}|\leq|b-a_{2}|,\quad(2):|b-a_{1}|\geq|b-a_{2}|.

By symmetry, we only consider the first case. Similar to (
5.60
) (see also figure
14
), we bound
{\cal L}^{(1)}
by the product of four
G
’s and
G^{\dagger}E_{b}G
as follows:

(5.65)

\displaystyle{\cal L}^{(1)}\leq W^{-2}\max_{x_{2}\in{\cal I}_{a_{2}}}\max_{y^{\prime}\in{\cal I}_{b^{\prime}}}\max_{x_{2}^{\prime}\in{\cal I}_{a^{\prime}_{2}}}\sum_{x_{1}\in{\cal I}_{a_{1}}}\sum_{x^{\prime}_{1}\in{\cal I}_{a^{\prime}_{1}}}\left|G_{x_{1}x_{2}}\right|\left|G_{x_{2}y^{\prime}}\right|\left|G_{y^{\prime}x_{2}^{\prime}}\right|\left|G_{x_{2}^{\prime}x_{1}^{\prime}}\right|\left|\left(G^{\dagger}E_{b}G\right)_{x_{1}x_{1}^{\prime}}\right|.

Using Schwarz’s inequality (on
\sum_{x_{1},x^{\prime}_{1}}
) and the definition
\cal L
, we have

(5.66)

\displaystyle{\cal L}^{(1)}\leq\max^{*}_{x_{1},x_{2},y^{\prime},x_{2},x_{2}^{\prime}}\left|G_{x_{1}x_{2}}\right|\left|G_{x_{2}y^{\prime}}\right|\left|G_{y^{\prime}x_{2}^{\prime}}\right|\left|G_{x_{2}^{\prime}x_{1}^{\prime}}\right|\cdot\left(\max_{\textbf{a}}\max_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}\in\{+,-\}^{4}}{\cal L}_{\boldsymbol{\sigma},a}\right)^{1/2},

where the
\max
is over the condition

{\bf 1}(x_{1}\in{\cal I}_{a_{1}}){\bf 1}(x_{2}\in{\cal I}_{a_{2}}){\bf 1}(y^{\prime}\in{\cal I}_{b^{\prime}}){\bf 1}(x^{\prime}_{1}\in{\cal I}_{a^{\prime}_{1}}){\bf 1}(x^{\prime}_{2}\in{\cal I}_{a^{\prime}_{2}}).

We claim that

(5.67)

\displaystyle|G_{y^{\prime}x_{2}}|\cdot|G_{y^{\prime}x_{2}^{\prime}}|\cdot|G_{x_{1}x_{2}}|\cdot|G_{x_{1}^{\prime}x_{2}^{\prime}}|\prec\left({\cal J}^{*}_{u,D}\right)^{2}\cdot{\cal T}_{t,D}(|a_{1}-a_{2}|)\cdot{\cal T}_{t,D}(|b-a_{2}|).

To prove this bound, we split it into two cases:

\displaystyle(1a):\;

\displaystyle\;|a_{1}-b|\leq\ell_{u}^{*},\quad\text{or}\quad|a_{1}^{\prime}-b|\leq\ell_{u}^{*}

(5.68)

\displaystyle(1b):\;

\displaystyle\;|a_{1}-b|\geq\ell_{u}^{*},\quad and\quad|a_{1}^{\prime}-b|\geq\ell_{u}^{*}.

Since
|a_{1}-a_{2}|\geq 4\ell_{t}^{*}
, we have, similar to (
5.61
), that

(5.69)

\displaystyle|G_{x_{1}x_{2}}|^{2}\leq

\displaystyle{\cal J}^{*}_{u,D}\cdot{\cal T}_{u,D}(|a_{1}-a_{2}|),

(5.70)

\displaystyle|G_{x_{1}^{\prime}x_{2}^{\prime}}|^{2}\leq

\displaystyle{\cal J}^{*}_{u,D}\cdot{\cal T}_{u,D}\left(|a_{1}^{\prime}-a_{2}^{\prime}|\right)\prec{\cal J}^{*}_{u,D}\cdot{\cal T}_{t,D}\left(|a_{1}-a_{2}|\right),

where we have used
{\cal T}_{u}\leq{\cal T}_{t}
, the assumption
|a_{i}^{\prime}-a_{i}|\leq\ell_{t}^{*}
and (
5.32
) in the second inequality. Thus we have

|G_{x_{1}x_{2}}|\cdot|G_{x_{1}^{\prime}x_{2}^{\prime}}|\prec{\cal J}^{*}_{u,D}\cdot{\cal T}_{t,D}\left(|a_{1}-a_{2}|\right).

For edges connecting with
b^{\prime}
, we have

|G_{y^{\prime}x_{2}}|\cdot|G_{y^{\prime}x_{2}^{\prime}}|\prec{\cal J}^{*}_{u,D}\cdot{\cal T}^{1/2}_{u,D}\left(|b-a_{2}|\right)\cdot{\cal T}^{1/2}_{u,D}\left(|b-a_{2}^{\prime}|\right),

where we have used that both
|b-a_{2}|
and
|b-a_{2}^{\prime}|
are larger than
\ell_{t}^{*}\geq\ell_{u}^{*}
.
Using
{\cal T}_{u}\leq{\cal T}_{t}
and
|a_{2}-a_{2}^{\prime}|\leq\ell_{t}^{*}
, we have

|G_{y^{\prime}x_{2}}|\cdot|G_{y^{\prime}x_{2}^{\prime}}|\leq{\cal J}^{*}_{u,D}\cdot{\cal T}_{t,D}\left(|b-a_{2}|\right).

Combining these bounds, we have proved (
5.67
).

Next, applying (
2.73
) on
\cal L
term in (
5.66
), together with (
5.67
) we obtain that

{\cal L}^{(1)}\prec(\ell_{u}/\ell_{s})^{3/2}\left(W\ell_{u}\eta_{u}\right)^{-3/2}\cdot\left({\cal J}^{*}_{u,D}\right)^{2}\cdot{\cal T}_{t,D}(|a_{1}-a_{2}|)\cdot{\cal T}_{t,D}(|b-a_{2}|).

Consider the case (1a) so that
|a_{2}-b|\geq|a_{1}-a_{2}|-\ell_{t}^{*}
. Therefore,
{\cal T}_{t,D}\left(|b-a_{2}|\right)\prec{\cal T}_{t,D}\left(|a_{1}-a_{2}|\right)
.
Combining these bounds with (
5.67
), we have

(5.71)

\displaystyle\sum_{b,b^{\prime}}^{(1a)}{\cal L}^{(1)}\prec

\displaystyle(\ell_{u}/\ell_{s})^{3/2}\cdot\ell_{u}\cdot\left(W\ell_{u}\eta_{u}\right)^{-3/2}\cdot\left({\cal J}^{*}_{u,D}\right)^{2}\cdot\left({\cal T}_{t,D}(|a_{1}-a_{2}|\right)^{2}.

For (1b), we bound
(G^{\dagger}E_{b}G)_{x_{1}x_{1}^{\prime}}
by

(G^{\dagger}E_{b}G)_{x_{1}x_{1}^{\prime}}\leq\max_{y\in{\cal I}_{b}}|G_{x_{1}y}||G_{x^{\prime}_{1}y}|.

Since
|a_{1}-b|,\;|a_{1}^{\prime}-b|\geq\ell_{u}^{*}
, we have, similar to (
5.61
),

(G^{\dagger}E_{b}G)_{x_{1}x_{1}^{\prime}}\prec{\cal J}^{*}_{u,D}\cdot{\cal T}_{t,D}(|b-a_{1}|).

Together with (
5.67
), we have

\displaystyle\sum_{b,b^{\prime}}^{(1b)}{\cal L}^{(1)}\prec\;

\displaystyle\left({\cal J}^{*}_{u,D}\right)^{3}\cdot{\cal T}_{t,D}(|a_{1}-a_{2}|)\cdot\sum_{b}{\cal T}_{t,D}(|b-a_{1}|){\cal T}_{t,D}(|b-a_{2}|)

(5.72)

\displaystyle\prec\;

\displaystyle\left({\cal J}^{*}_{u,D}\right)^{3}\cdot\ell_{t}\cdot(W\ell_{t}\eta_{t})^{-2}\cdot\left({\cal T}_{t,D}(|a_{1}-a_{2}|)\right)^{2}.

Here the last line was bounded as in the proof of (
5.50
). Putting the bounds for (1a) and (1b) together, we obtain (
5.7
) and complete the proof of Lemma
5.7
.
∎

Proof of (
2.75
).

Combining the
\cal L-\cal K
estimate (
2.76
) and the
\cal K
bound in Lemma
2.17
, we obtain that

(5.73)

\max_{a,b}{\cal L}_{u,(+,-),(a,b)}\prec\left(\eta_{s}/\eta_{u}\right)^{4}\cdot(W\ell_{u}\eta_{u})^{-2}+(W\ell_{u}\eta_{u})^{-1}\prec(W\ell_{u}\eta_{u})^{-1},\quad u\in[s,t]

where we have used the inductive assumption (
2.72
).
Then we can apply the resolvent entry estimate in Lemma
4.1
. The weak local law in (
2.74
) implies the assumptions of Lemma
4.1
. From (
4.3
), (
4.2
) and (
5.73
), we have

\|G_{t}-m\|^{2}_{\max}\prec\max_{a,b}{\cal L}_{u,(+,-),(a,b)}\prec(W\ell_{u}\eta_{u})^{-1}.

This completes the proof of (
2.75
).
∎

5.4
Fast decay property

In the second step of the proof for Theorem
2.21
, we established the decay property of the
2\text{-}G
loop in (
2.76
). In this subsection, we extend this decay property to general loops
\mathcal{L}
and
\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K}
. This decay property proves to be highly useful for bounding the
\mathcal{E}
terms in equation (
5.3
). We begin by defining the
\ell_{u}
-decay property.

Definition 5.8

(The decay property)
.

Let
\cal A
be a tensor
\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}
.We say
\cal A
has
(u,\tau,D)
decay at the time
u
if for some fixed small
\tau>0
and large
D>0
, we have

(5.74)

\max_{i}\|a_{i}-a_{j}\|\geq\ell_{u}W^{\tau}\implies{\cal A}_{\textbf{a}}=O(W^{-D}),\quad\textbf{a}=(a_{1},a_{2}\cdots,a_{n}).

Lemma 5.9

(The decay property of
{\cal L}_{u}
)
.

Assume that (
2.75
) and (
2.76
) hold. Then for
any
n\geq 2
, any small
\tau
, large
D
and
D^{\prime}>0
, the
{\cal L}_{u}
has
(u,\tau,D)
decay with probability
1-O(W^{-D^{\prime}})
. More precisely,

(5.75)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\Big(\left|{\cal L}_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\right|+\left|{(\cal L-K})_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\right|\Big)\cdot{\bf 1}\left(\max_{i,j}\|a_{i}-a_{j}\|\geq\ell_{u}W^{\tau}\right)\geq W^{-D}\right)\leq W^{-D^{\prime}}.

Proof of Lemma
5.9
.

Combining the decay properties of
{\cal K}_{u,(+,-)}
and
\cal L-\cal K
(
2.76
), we obtain that

{\cal L}_{u,(+,-)}
has
(u,\tau,D)
decay property for any fixed
(\tau,D)
with high probability.
Applying the
G_{ij}
estimate in (
4.2
) and the new
{\cal L}_{u}
decay property, we have

\forall\tau,D,D^{\prime},\quad\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{i\in{\cal I}_{a_{1}}}\max_{j\in{\cal I}_{a_{2}}}|G_{ij}|\cdot{\bf 1}\left(|a_{1}-a_{2}\|\geq\ell_{u}W^{\tau}\right)\geq W^{-D}\right)\leq W^{-D^{\prime}}.

By definition
{\cal L}=\langle\prod\limits_{i=1}^{n}G_{i}E_{a_{i}}\rangle
has the
(u,\tau,D)
decay property. On the other hand, by the decay of
\Theta^{(B)}_{t}
and the tree representation of
\cal K
in Lemma
3.5
,
\cal K
also has
(u,\tau,D)
decay property. Therefore
({\cal L-\cal K})_{u}
has
(u,\tau,D)
decay property.

∎

The decay property enables us to use Lemma
7.3
for
\|{\cal U}_{u,t}\|_{\max\to\max}
on fast decay tensors. This provides a major improvement over using Lemma
7.1
to bound
\|{\cal U}_{u,t}\|_{\max\to\max}
. We have the following lemma estimating
\cal E
terms.

Lemma 5.10

(Bounds on
\cal E
terms)
.

Assume that (
2.75
) and (
2.76
) hold.
Define
\Xi^{({\cal L})}
and
\Xi^{({\cal L}-{\cal K})}
as follows

(5.76)

\displaystyle\Xi^{({\cal L})}_{t,m}

\displaystyle:=\max_{\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\left|{\cal L}_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\right|\cdot\left(W\ell_{t}\eta_{t}\right)^{m-1}\cdot{\bf 1}(\boldsymbol{\sigma}\in\{+,-\}^{m}),

\displaystyle\Xi^{({\cal L}-{\cal K})}_{t,m}

\displaystyle:=\max_{\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\left|({\cal L}-{\cal K})_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\right|\cdot\left(W\ell_{t}\eta_{t}\right)^{m}\cdot{\bf 1}(\boldsymbol{\sigma}\in\{+,-\}^{m}).

Then

\displaystyle\big[\mathcal{K}\sim(\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K})\big]_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}^{l_{\mathcal{K}}}\;\prec\;

\displaystyle\left(\max_{k<n}\Xi^{(\cal L-\cal K)}_{u,k}\right)\cdot(W\ell_{u}\eta_{u})^{-n}\cdot\frac{1}{\eta_{u}},

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}^{((\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K})\times(\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K}))}\;\prec\;

\displaystyle\left(\max_{k:\;2\leq k\leq n}\Xi^{(\cal L-\cal K)}_{u,k}\cdot\Xi^{(\cal L-\cal K)}_{u,n-k+2}\cdot(W\ell_{u}\eta_{u})^{-1}\right)\cdot(W\ell_{u}\eta_{u})^{-n}\cdot\frac{1}{\eta_{u}},

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}^{(\widetilde{G})}\;\prec\;

\displaystyle\Xi^{(\cal L)}_{u,n+1}\cdot(W\ell_{u}\eta_{u})^{-n}\cdot\frac{1}{\eta_{u}},

(5.77)

\displaystyle(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{E})_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\;\prec\;

\displaystyle\;\Xi^{(\cal L)}_{u,{2n+2}}\cdot(W\ell_{u}\eta_{u})^{-2n}\cdot\frac{1}{\eta_{u}}.

Furthermore,
all these
\cal E
terms have the
(u,\tau,D)
decay property for
u\in[s,t]
.

Proof of Lemma
5.10
.

The proof follows directly the definitions of these terms and the decay properties in Lemma
5.9
.

•

By definition (
5.2
),
\mathcal{K}\sim(\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K})
can be written as the product of
\cal K
and
\cal L-\cal K
loops. Bounding
\cal K
with (
2.59
), we have

(5.78)

\displaystyle\big[\mathcal{K}\sim(\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K})\big]_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}^{l_{\mathcal{K}}}\;\prec\;

\displaystyle W\cdot(W\ell_{u}\eta_{u})^{-l_{\mathcal{K}}+1}\cdot\ell_{u}\cdot\Xi^{(\cal L-\cal K)}_{u,(n-l_{\mathcal{K}}+2)}\cdot(W\ell_{u}\eta_{u})^{-(n-l_{\mathcal{K}}+2)}

where we used
l_{\mathcal{K}}\geq 3
and the
\ell_{u}
factor comes from the sum of the index
a
, which was restricted by the decay property of
\mathcal{K}
.
As a side remark, the other index
b
was restricted by
S^{(B)}_{ab}
.

•

By definition (
5.13
),
\mathcal{E}^{((\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K})\times(\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K}))}
can be written as the product of two loops, whose total length is
n+2
.

(5.79)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{((\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K})\times(\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K}))}\;\prec\;

\displaystyle W\cdot\sum_{k=2}^{n}\Xi^{(\cal L-\cal K)}_{u,\,k}\cdot(W\ell_{u}\eta_{u})^{-k}\cdot\ell_{u}\cdot\Xi^{(\cal L-\cal K)}_{u,(n-k+2)}\cdot(W\ell_{u}\eta_{u})^{-(n-k+2)}

•

By definition (
2.47
),

\mathcal{E}^{(\widetilde{G})}
can be written as the product of a
(\cal L-\cal K)
loop of length one and an
(n+1)-G
loop. With (
4.5
), we have

(5.80)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{(\widetilde{G})}\;\prec\;

\displaystyle W\cdot\Xi^{\cal L}_{u,\,2}\cdot(W\ell_{u}\eta_{u})^{-1}\cdot\ell_{u}\cdot\Xi^{\cal L}_{u,(n+1)}\cdot(W\ell_{u}\eta_{u})^{-n}

Furthermore, with (
2.76
), we can bound
\Xi^{\cal L}_{u,\,2}\prec 1
.

•

By definition (
5.4
),

(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{E})
can be written in terms of
(2n+2)
-
G
-loops. Thus

(5.81)

\displaystyle(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{E})\prec\Xi^{(\cal L)}_{u,{2n+2}}\cdot W\cdot\ell_{u}\cdot(W\ell_{u}\eta_{u})^{-2n-1}.

Here all
\ell_{u}
factors come from summing an index restricted to a range
\ell_{u}
.
∎

In the remaining of this subsection, we will use Lemma
5.10
to improve estimates on
\cal L-\cal K
.

Lemma 5.11
.

Assume that
\boldsymbol{\sigma}
is not an alternating sign vector, i.e.,

(5.82)

\exists k,\;s.t.\;\sigma_{k}=\sigma_{k+1}.

Suppose that the assumptions of Theorem
2.21
, the local law (
2.75
) and the decay property for
\cal L-\cal K
(
2.76
) hold. If

\max_{u\in[s,t]}\,\Xi^{(\cal L)}_{u,{2n+2}}\prec{\Lambda}

for some deterministic quantity
\Lambda\geq 1
,
then

(5.83)

\displaystyle\Xi^{({\cal L-\cal K})}_{u,n}\;\prec\;

\displaystyle\;{\Lambda}^{1/2}+\max_{u\in[s,t]}\left(\;\max_{k<n}\Xi^{({\cal L-\cal K})}_{u,k}+\max_{k:\;2\leq k\leq n}\Xi^{({\cal L-\cal K})}_{u,k}\cdot\Xi^{({\cal L-\cal K})}_{u,n-k+2}\cdot(W\ell_{u}\eta_{u})^{-1}+\Xi^{({\cal L})}_{u,n+1}\right).

Proof of Lemma
5.11
.

By case 1 of (
7.16
),

\big\|{\cal U}_{u,t,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\big\|_{\max\to\max}\Big|({\rm fast\;decay\;tensorsatisfying\eqref{NALsigm}})\leq C_{n}W^{C_{n}\tau}\cdot\left(\frac{\ell_{u}\eta_{u}}{\ell_{t}\eta_{t}}\right)^{n}.

By assumptions of
Theorem
2.21
on
({\cal L-K})_{s}
, the
\cal E
estimates in Lemma
5.10
, and the previous bound on
\max\to\max
norm of
{\cal U}_{u,t}
,
we have

\displaystyle(W\ell_{t}\eta_{t})^{n}\cdot(\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K})_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\;\prec\;\;

\displaystyle 1+\int_{s}^{t}\left(\max_{k<n}\Xi^{({\cal L-\cal K})}_{u,k}\right)\cdot\frac{1}{\eta_{u}}du

\displaystyle+\int_{s}^{t}\left(\max_{k:\;2\leq k\leq n}\Xi^{({\cal L-\cal K})}_{u,k}\cdot\Xi^{({\cal L-\cal K})}_{u,n-k+2}\cdot(W\ell_{u}\eta_{u})^{-1}\right)\cdot\frac{1}{\eta_{u}}du

(5.84)

\displaystyle+\int_{s}^{t}\Xi^{(\cal L)}_{u,n+1}\cdot\frac{1}{\eta_{u}}du+(W\ell_{t}\eta_{t})^{n}\cdot\int_{s}^{t}\left(\mathcal{U}_{u,t,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\circ\mathcal{E}^{(M)}_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\right)_{\textbf{a}}du.

By Lemma
5.5
, we know that

(5.85)

\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{s}^{t}\left(\mathcal{U}_{u,t,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\circ\mathcal{E}^{(M)}_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\right)_{\textbf{a}}\right]^{2p}\leq C_{n,p}\;\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{s}^{t}\left(\left(\mathcal{U}_{u,t,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\otimes\mathcal{U}_{u,t,\overline{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}\right)\;\circ\;\left(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{E}\right)_{u,\,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\right)_{{\textbf{a}},{\textbf{a}}}du\right)^{p}.

Using (
5.10
) to bound
\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{E}
, we have

(5.86)

\displaystyle(W\ell_{t}\eta_{t})^{2n}\cdot\int_{s}^{t}\left(\mathcal{U}_{u,t,(\boldsymbol{\sigma},\overline{\boldsymbol{\sigma}})}\;\circ\;\left(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{E}\right)_{u,\,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\right)_{{\textbf{a}},{\textbf{a}}}du\prec\int_{s}^{t}\Xi^{(\cal L)}_{u,{2n+2}}\cdot\frac{1}{\eta_{u}}du.

This completes the proof of Lemma
5.11
.

∎

5.5
Sum zero operator
{\cal Q}_{t}

In this subsection, we introduce another key tool,
{\cal Q}_{t}
, for estimating the loop hierarchy.

Definition 5.12

(Definition of
{\cal Q}_{t}
)
.

Let
{\cal A}_{\textbf{a}}
be a tensor with
\textbf{a}\in\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{n}
,
n\geq 2
. Define
{\cal P}
by

\left({\cal P}\circ{\cal A}\right)_{a_{1}}=\sum_{a_{2},\,a_{3}\cdots a_{n}}{\cal A}_{\textbf{a}},\quad\textbf{a}=(a_{1},\cdots,a_{n}),

where the index
a_{1}
was fixed. A tensor
A
has a sum zero property if and only if
{\cal P}\circ{\cal A}=0
. Define

\left({\cal Q}_{t}\circ{\cal A}\right)_{\textbf{a}}={\cal A}_{\textbf{a}}-\left({\cal P}\circ{\cal A}\right)_{a_{1}}\cdot{\vartheta}_{t,\,\textbf{a}},\quad{\vartheta}_{t,\textbf{a}}:=\big(1-t\big)^{n-1}\prod_{i=2}^{n}\left(\Theta^{(B)}_{t}\right)_{a_{1}a_{i}}.

Since
\sum_{b}\left(\Theta^{(B)}_{t}\right)_{ab}=(1-t)^{-1}
, it is easy to check that
{\cal P}\circ\vartheta_{t,\textbf{a}}=1

and
{\cal P}\circ{\cal Q}_{t}=0
.

The reason for using
\vartheta_{t,\textbf{a}}
in the definition of
{\cal Q}_{t}
is to preserve both the
\max
norm and the rapid decay properties of
{\cal A}
, provided that
{\cal A}
possesses such properties.

Lemma 5.13

(Properties
{\cal Q}_{t}
)
.

Let
\cal A
be a tensor
\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}
. Assume that
\cal A
has (
t,\tau,D
) decay property. Then

(5.87)

\displaystyle\max_{\textbf{a}}\left({\cal Q}_{t}\circ\cal A\right)_{\textbf{a}}\leq W^{C_{n}\tau}\cdot\max_{\textbf{a}}{\cal A}_{\textbf{a}}+W^{-D+C_{n}}.

Furthermore,

\left({\cal Q}_{t}\circ\cal A\right)_{\textbf{a}}
has the (
t,\tau,D
) decay property.

We now use
{\cal Q}_{t}
to improve our estimates on
{\cal L}-{\cal K}
integral representation (
5.3
).
Denote by
\dot{\vartheta}_{t,\textbf{a}}=\frac{d}{dt}\vartheta_{t,\textbf{a}}
.
From the hierarchy of
\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K}
(
5.15
), we have

(5.88)

\displaystyle d{\cal Q}_{t}\circ(\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K})_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}=

\displaystyle{\cal Q}_{t}\circ\Big[\mathcal{K}\sim(\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K})\Big]^{l_{\mathcal{K}}=2}_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}+\sum_{l_{\mathcal{K}}>2}{\cal Q}_{t}\circ\Big[\mathcal{K}\sim(\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K})\Big]^{l_{\mathcal{K}}}_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}

\displaystyle+{\cal Q}_{t}\circ\mathcal{E}^{((\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K})\times(\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K}))}_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}+{\cal Q}_{t}\circ\mathcal{E}^{(M)}_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}+{\cal Q}_{t}\circ\mathcal{E}^{(\widetilde{G})}_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}+\left[{\cal P}\circ\left(\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K}\right)_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\cdot\dot{\vartheta}_{t}\right]_{\textbf{a}}.

To match the form appearing in Lemma
5.3
, we write the first term on the right-hand side as

(5.89)

\displaystyle{\cal Q}_{t}\circ\Big[\mathcal{K}\sim(\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K})\Big]^{l_{\mathcal{K}}=2}_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}

\displaystyle={\cal Q}_{t}\circ\varTheta_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\circ(\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K})_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}

\displaystyle=\varTheta_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\circ{\cal Q}_{t}\circ(\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K})_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}+\big[{\cal Q}_{t},\varTheta_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\big]\circ(\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K})_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}},

where
\big[{\cal Q}_{t},\varTheta_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\big]
is the commutator
of
{\cal Q}_{t}
and
\varTheta_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\big]
.
Since
{\cal P}\circ{\cal Q}_{t}=0
, the left-hand side of (
5.89
) satisfies the sum-zero property. Furthermore, with the definition of
\varTheta_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}
in Definition
5.2
, one can verify that if
{\cal A}
has the sum-zero property, so does
\varTheta_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\circ{\cal A}
, i.e.,

{\cal P}\circ{\cal A}=0\implies{\cal P}\circ\varTheta_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\circ{\cal A}=0.

It implies the first term in the right hand side of (
5.89
) has the sum-zero property. Since the left hand side of (
5.89
) also has this property, we obtain that the last term in (
5.89
) has sum zero property too, i.e.,

(5.90)

\displaystyle{\cal P}\circ\big[{\cal Q}_{t},\varTheta_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\big]\circ(\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K})_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}=0.

Then combining (
5.88
) and (
5.89
), and applying Lemma
5.3
, we obtain the following equation, paralleling to (
5.3
),

\displaystyle{\cal Q}_{t}\circ(\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K})_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\;=\;\;

\displaystyle\left(\mathcal{U}_{s,t,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\circ{\cal Q}_{s}\circ(\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K})_{s,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\right)_{\textbf{a}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{l_{\mathcal{K}}>2}\int_{s}^{t}\left(\mathcal{U}_{u,t,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\circ{\cal Q}_{u}\circ\Big[\mathcal{K}\sim(\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K})\Big]^{l_{\mathcal{K}}}_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\right)_{\textbf{a}}du

\displaystyle+\int_{s}^{t}\left(\mathcal{U}_{u,t,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\circ{\cal Q}_{u}\circ\mathcal{E}^{((\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K})\times(\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K}))}_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\right)_{\textbf{a}}du

\displaystyle+\int_{s}^{t}\left(\mathcal{U}_{u,t,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\circ{\cal Q}_{u}\circ\mathcal{E}^{(\widetilde{G})}_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\right)_{\textbf{a}}du+\int_{s}^{t}\left(\mathcal{U}_{u,t,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\circ{\cal Q}_{u}\circ\mathcal{E}^{(M)}_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\right)_{\textbf{a}}du

(5.91)

\displaystyle+\int_{s}^{t}\left(\mathcal{U}_{u,t,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\circ\left(\big[{\cal Q}_{u},\varTheta_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\big]\circ(\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K})_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\right)\right)_{\textbf{a}}du

\displaystyle+\int_{s}^{t}\left(\mathcal{U}_{u,t,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\circ\left[{\cal P}\circ\left(\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K}\right)_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\cdot\dot{\vartheta}_{u}\right]\right)_{\textbf{a}}du.

In the next lemma, we will utilize the fact that all terms on the right-hand side of (
5.5
) exhibit both fast decay and sum-zero properties.

Lemma 5.14
.

Suppose that the assumptions of
Theorem
2.21
, the local law (
2.75
) and the decay property of
\cal L-\cal K
(
2.76
) hold.
If

\max_{u\in[s,t]}\,\Xi^{(\cal L)}_{u,{2n+2}}\prec{\Lambda}

for some deterministic quantity
\Lambda\geq 1
,
then

(5.92)

\displaystyle\Xi^{({\cal L-\cal K})}_{u,n}\;\prec\;

\displaystyle\;{\Lambda}^{1/2}+\max_{u\in[s,t]}\left(\;\max_{k<n}\Xi^{({\cal L-\cal K})}_{u,k}+\max_{k:\;2\leq k\leq n}\Xi^{({\cal L-\cal K})}_{u,k}\cdot\Xi^{({\cal L-\cal K})}_{u,n-k+2}\cdot(W\ell_{u}\eta_{u})^{-1}+\Xi^{({\cal L})}_{u,n+1}\right).

Notice that there is no prefactor depending on
(\eta_{s}/\eta_{t})
on the right hand side of the last inequality.

Proof of Lemma
5.14
.

In the previous subsection, we have proved (
5.92
) for the non-alternating case, i.e., (
5.82
). Hence we only need to focus on the alternating case

\forall\;k\quad\sigma_{k}=\overline{\sigma}_{k+1}
.
By Lemma
5.13
,

{\cal Q}_{s}\;\circ({\cal L-K})_{s},\quad{\cal Q}\;\circ({\cal K\sim L-K}),\quad{\cal Q}\;\circ{\cal E}^{\cal(L-K)\times(L-K)},\quad{\cal Q}\;\circ{\cal E}^{(\widetilde{G})}

have
the sum zero and fast decay properties. Furthermore, their
\max
norms can be bounded with (
5.87
), which amounts to

\|{\cal Q}\|_{\max\to\max}\prec 1
.
By (
7.16
), we have the following bound for sum zero fast decay tensors:

(5.93)

\displaystyle\big\|{\cal U}_{u,t,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\big\|_{\max\to\max}\Big|({\rm fast\;decay\;and\;sum\;zero\;tensor})\leq C_{n}W^{C_{n}\tau}\cdot\left(\frac{\ell_{u}\eta_{u}}{\ell_{t}\eta_{t}}\right)^{n}.

Following the same argument in the proof of Lemma
5.11
, we have

\displaystyle(W\ell_{t}\eta_{t})^{n}\cdot{\cal Q}_{t}\circ(\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K})_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\;\prec\;\;

\displaystyle\;1+\max_{u\in[s,t]}\left(\;\max_{k<n}\Xi^{({\cal L-\cal K})}_{u,k}+\max_{2\leq k\leq n}\Xi^{({\cal L-\cal K})}_{u,k}\cdot\Xi^{({\cal L-\cal K})}_{u,n-k+2}\cdot(W\ell_{u}\eta_{u})^{-1}+\Xi^{({\cal L})}_{u,n+1}\right)

\displaystyle\;+\;

\displaystyle\;(W\ell_{t}\eta_{t})^{n}\cdot\int_{s}^{t}\left(\mathcal{U}_{u,t,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\circ Q_{t}\circ\mathcal{E}^{(M)}_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\right)_{\textbf{a}}du

(5.94)

\displaystyle\;+\;

\displaystyle\;(W\ell_{t}\eta_{t})^{n}\int_{s}^{t}\left(\mathcal{U}_{u,t,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\circ\left(\big[{\cal Q}_{u},\varTheta_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\big]\circ(\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K})_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\right)\right)_{\textbf{a}}du

\displaystyle\;+\;

\displaystyle\;(W\ell_{t}\eta_{t})^{n}\int_{s}^{t}\left(\mathcal{U}_{u,t,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\circ\left[{\cal P}\circ\left(\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K}\right)_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\cdot\dot{\vartheta}_{u}\right]\right)_{\textbf{a}}du.

Next, we estimate the last line in (
5.5
). Since
{\cal P}\circ\vartheta_{t,\textbf{a}}=1
, we have
{\cal P}\circ\dot{\vartheta}_{t,\textbf{a}}=0
and

{\cal P}\circ\left[{\cal P}\circ\left(\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K}\right)_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\cdot\dot{\vartheta}_{u}\right]_{\textbf{a}}=\left({\cal P}\circ\left(\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K}\right)_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\right)_{a_{1}}{\cal P}\circ\dot{\vartheta}_{u,\textbf{a}}=0.

Therefore, this term also has the sum zero property. From its definition,
\vartheta
has a fast decay property. Then with (
7.16
) (case 2), we have

(5.95)

\displaystyle\int_{s}^{t}\left(\mathcal{U}_{u,t,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\circ\left[{\cal P}\circ\left(\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K}\right)_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\cdot\dot{\vartheta}_{u}\right]\right)_{\textbf{a}}du\;\prec\;

\displaystyle\;\int_{s}^{t}\left(\frac{\ell_{u}\eta_{u}}{\ell_{t}\eta_{t}}\right)^{n}\cdot\max_{a}\left|\left[{\cal P}\circ\left(\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K}\right)_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\right]_{a}\right|\max_{\textbf{a}}\left|\dot{\vartheta}_{u,\textbf{a}}\right|du.

We claim that for non-constant
\boldsymbol{\sigma}
, i.e.,
\{\sigma_{1},\cdots,\sigma_{n}\}=\{+,-\}
, the following holds:

(5.96)

\displaystyle\left[{\cal P}\circ\left(\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K}\right)_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\right]_{a}\prec(W\eta_{u})^{-n}\ell_{u}^{-1}\cdot\Xi^{(\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K})}_{u,n-1}

for any loop length
n
. To see this, for a fixed non-constant
\boldsymbol{\sigma}
, there exists
1<k\leq n
such that
\sigma_{k}=\overline{\sigma}_{k+1}
(i.e., a pair of opposite charges). By Ward’s identity (Lemma
3.6
), we can sum
\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K}
of rank
n
over the index
a_{k}
and express the resulting sum in terms of
\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K}
of rank
n-1
with a multiplicative factor
(W\eta_{u})^{-1}
. The summation over the remaining indices contributes an additional factor of
\ell_{u}^{n-2}
due to the fast decay property.

On the other hand, with the definition of
\vartheta
and
\Theta^{(B)}
, we have

\max_{\textbf{a}}\left|\dot{\vartheta}_{u,\textbf{a}}\right|\prec\ell_{u}^{-n+1}\cdot\eta_{u}^{-1}.

Inserting them back to (
5.95
), we obtain that

(5.97)

\displaystyle\int_{s}^{t}\left(\mathcal{U}_{u,t,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\circ\left[{\cal P}\circ\left(\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K}\right)_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\cdot\dot{\vartheta}_{u}\right]\right)_{\textbf{a}}du\prec(W\ell_{t}\eta_{t})^{-n}\max_{u\in[s,t]}\Xi^{(\cal L-K)}_{u,\;n-1}.

Similarly, we can estimate the second line of (
5.5
). By definition of
\varTheta
and Lemma
5.13
,
{\varTheta}_{u}\circ{\cal A}
and
{\cal Q}_{u}\circ{\cal A}

are fast decay if
{\cal A}_{u}
is fast decay. Since
(\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K})_{u}
has fast decay property at time
u
, so does
\left[\mathcal{Q}_{u},\Theta_{u,\sigma}\right]\circ(\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K})
. On the other hand, we just showed that
\left[\mathcal{Q}_{u},\Theta_{u,\sigma}\right]\circ(\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K})
also has sum zero property from (
5.90
).
Therefore, with (
7.16
) (case 2), we have

(5.98)

\displaystyle\int_{s}^{t}\left(\mathcal{U}_{u,t,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\circ\left(\big[{\cal Q}_{u},\varTheta_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\big]\circ(\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K})_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\right)\right)_{\textbf{a}}du\;\prec\;

\displaystyle\;\int_{s}^{t}\left(\frac{\ell_{u}\eta_{u}}{\ell_{t}\eta_{t}}\right)^{n}\cdot\max_{a}\left|\left(\big[{\cal Q}_{u},\varTheta_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\big]\circ(\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K})_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\right)_{\textbf{a}}\right|du.

By definition, we can bound

(5.99)

\displaystyle\big[{\cal Q}_{u},\varTheta_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\big]\circ(\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K})_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\;=\;

\displaystyle\;\left[\left({\cal P}\circ\left(\varTheta_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\circ(\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K})\right)\right)\cdot\vartheta_{u}\right]_{\textbf{a}}-\varTheta_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\circ\left[{\cal P}\circ\left(\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K}\right)\cdot\vartheta_{t}\right]_{\textbf{a}}.

\displaystyle\;\prec\;

\displaystyle\;\|\vartheta_{u}\|_{\max}\|\cdot\eta_{u}^{-1}\max_{a}\left[{\cal P}\circ\left(\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K}\right)_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\right]_{a}.

Inserting it back to (
5.98
) and using (
5.96
), we obtain that

(5.100)

\displaystyle\int_{s}^{t}\left(\mathcal{U}_{u,t,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\circ\left(\big[{\cal Q}_{u},\varTheta_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\big]\circ(\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K})_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\right)\right)_{\textbf{a}}du\;\prec(W\ell_{t}\eta_{t})^{-n}\max_{u\in[s,t]}\Xi^{(\cal L-K)}_{u,\;n-1}.

Furthermore, we can write the left hand side of (
5.5
) as

(5.101)

\displaystyle{\cal Q}_{t}\circ(\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K})_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}=(\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K})_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}-{\cal P}\circ(\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K})_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\cdot\vartheta_{\textbf{a}}=(\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K})_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}+O_{\prec}\left((W\ell_{t}\eta_{t})^{-n}\Xi^{(\cal L-K)}_{t,\;n-1}\right).

Inserting (
5.97
), (
5.100
) and (
5.101
) into (
5.5
), we obtain

\displaystyle(W\ell_{t}\eta_{t})^{n}\cdot(\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K})_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\;\prec\;\;

\displaystyle\;1+\max_{u\in[s,t]}\left(\;\max_{k<n}\Xi^{({\cal L-\cal K})}_{u,k}+\max_{k:\;2\leq k\leq n}\Xi^{({\cal L-\cal K})}_{u,k}\cdot\Xi^{({\cal L-\cal K})}_{u,n-k+2}\cdot(W\ell_{u}\eta_{u})^{-1}+\Xi^{({\cal L})}_{u,n+1}\right)

(5.102)

\displaystyle\;+\;

\displaystyle\;(W\ell_{t}\eta_{t})^{n}\cdot\int_{s}^{t}\left(\mathcal{U}_{u,t,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\circ{\cal Q}_{t}\circ\mathcal{E}^{(M)}_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\right)_{\textbf{a}}du.

For the martingale term, one can easily derive that

\displaystyle\left[\int\left(\mathcal{U}_{u,t,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\circ{\cal Q}_{u}\circ\mathcal{E}^{(M)}_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\right)_{\textbf{a}}\right]_{t}\;=\;

\displaystyle\int_{s}^{t}\sum_{\alpha}\left|\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathcal{U}_{u,t,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\circ{\cal Q}_{u}\circ\mathcal{E}^{(M)}_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}(\alpha,k)\right|^{2}du

\displaystyle\;\leq\;

\displaystyle C_{n}\;\int_{s}^{t}\sum_{\alpha}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left|\mathcal{U}_{u,t,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\circ{\cal Q}_{u}\circ\mathcal{E}^{(M)}_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}(\alpha,k)\right|^{2}du

(5.103)

\displaystyle\;=\;

\displaystyle C_{n}\;\int_{s}^{t}\left(\left(\mathcal{U}_{u,t,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\otimes\mathcal{U}_{u,t,\overline{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}\right)\circ\left(\mathcal{Q}_{u}\otimes\mathcal{Q}_{u}\right)\circ\left(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{E}\right)_{u,\,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\right)_{\textbf{a},\textbf{a}}du,

where

\displaystyle\left(\left(\mathcal{Q}_{u}\otimes\mathcal{Q}_{u}\right)\circ{\cal A}\right)_{\textbf{a},\textbf{b}}\;=\;

\displaystyle\;{\cal A}_{\textbf{a},\textbf{b}}-\delta_{a_{1}^{\prime},a_{1}}\sum_{a_{2}^{\prime}\cdots a_{n}^{\prime}}{\cal A}_{\textbf{a}^{\prime},\,\textbf{b}}\cdot\vartheta_{u,\textbf{a}}-\delta_{b_{1}^{\prime},b_{1}}\sum_{b_{2}^{\prime}\cdots b_{n}^{\prime}}{\cal A}_{\textbf{a},\textbf{b}^{\prime}}\cdot\vartheta_{u,\textbf{b}}

(5.104)

\displaystyle\;+\;

\displaystyle\;\delta_{a_{1}^{\prime},a_{1}}\delta_{b_{1}^{\prime},b_{1}}\sum_{a^{\prime}_{2}\cdots a^{\prime}_{n}}\sum_{b_{2}^{\prime}\cdots b_{n}^{\prime}}{\cal A}_{\textbf{a}^{\prime},\textbf{b}^{\prime}}\cdot\vartheta_{u,\textbf{a}}\cdot\vartheta_{u,\textbf{b}},

where

\textbf{a}=(a_{1},\cdots,a_{n}),\quad\textbf{a}^{\prime}=(a^{\prime}_{1},\cdots,a^{\prime}_{n})
and similarly for

b
and
\textbf{b}^{\prime}
. By definition of
\vartheta
, it is easy to check that

\sum_{a_{2},\cdots,a_{n}}\sum_{b_{1}\cdots b_{n}}\left(\left(\mathcal{Q}_{u}\otimes\mathcal{Q}_{u}\right)\circ{\cal A}\right)_{\textbf{a},\textbf{b}}=0.

Using (
5.93
) on the fast decay tensor
\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{E}
, we have

\displaystyle\left(\left(\mathcal{U}_{u,t,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\otimes\mathcal{U}_{u,t,\overline{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}\right)\circ\left(\mathcal{Q}_{u}\otimes\mathcal{Q}_{u}\right)\circ\left(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{E}\right)_{u,\,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\right)_{\textbf{a},\textbf{a}}\;\prec\;\;\left(\frac{\ell_{u}\eta_{u}}{\ell_{t}\eta_{t}}\right)^{2n}\max_{\textbf{a},\textbf{a}^{\prime}}\left(\left(\mathcal{Q}_{u}\otimes\mathcal{Q}_{u}\right)\circ\left(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{E}\right)_{u,\,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\right)_{\textbf{a},\textbf{a}^{\prime}}

(5.105)

\displaystyle\;\prec\;

\displaystyle\;\left(\frac{\ell_{u}\eta_{u}}{\ell_{t}\eta_{t}}\right)^{2n}\max_{\textbf{a},\textbf{a}^{\prime}}\left(\left(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{E}\right)_{u,\,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\right)_{\textbf{a},\textbf{a}^{\prime}}\;\prec\;\;\left(W\ell_{t}\eta_{t}\right)^{-2n}\cdot\eta_{u}^{-1}\cdot\Xi^{\cal L}_{u,2n+2},

where we have used (
5.10
). Inserting it back to (
5.5
) and using BDG inequality and the assumption
\Xi^{\cal L}_{u,2n+2}\prec\Lambda
, we obtain that

\left(W\ell_{t}\eta_{t}\right)^{n}\cdot\int_{s}^{t}\left(\mathcal{U}_{u,t,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\circ{\cal Q}_{u}\circ\mathcal{E}_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}^{(\mathrm{M})}\right)_{\mathbf{a}}du\prec\Lambda^{1/2}.

This completes the proof of Lemma
5.14
.

∎

5.6
Proof of Theorem
2.21
, step 3

Recall that in this step, in addition to the assumptions in Theorem
2.21
, we also have the local law (
2.75
) and the decay property for
\cal L-\cal K
(
2.76
). With these inputs, we obtained (
5.92
) in Lemma
5.14
. In this step, we aim to prove the correct bound (
2.77
) on
G
-loop.
We have proved in Step 1 the following bounds

(5.106)

\displaystyle\Xi^{(\cal L)}_{u,n}=(\ell_{u}/\ell_{s})^{n-1},\quad\Xi^{({\cal L-K})}_{u,n}\prec(W\ell_{u}\eta_{u})\cdot(\ell_{u}/\ell_{s})^{n-1}.

Using the bound (
2.59
) on
\cal K
, we can relate
\Xi^{(\cal L)}
with
\Xi^{(\cal L-\cal K)}
as follows:

(5.107)

\displaystyle\Xi^{(\cal L)}_{u,n}\prec 1+(W\ell_{u}\eta_{u})^{-1}\cdot\Xi^{({\cal L-\cal K})}_{u,n},\quad\quad\Xi^{({\cal L-\cal K})}_{u,n}\prec(W\ell_{u}\eta_{u})\left(\Xi^{(\cal L)}_{u,n}+1\right).

Denote by

(5.108)

\Psi(n,k,s,u,t):=(W\ell_{s}\eta_{s})^{1/2}+(\ell_{t}/\ell_{s})^{n-1}\times\begin{cases}(W\ell_{u}\eta_{u})^{1-k/4}&k=0\\
(W\ell_{s}\eta_{s})^{1-k/4}&k\geq 1\end{cases},\quad u\in[s,t].

We say that the estimate
{\cal S}(n,k,s,u,t)
holds if
\Xi^{({\cal L-K})}_{u,n}\prec\Psi(n,k,s,u,t)
.
We will prove that for any
n\geq 3
and
k\geq 1

(5.109)

\displaystyle{\cal S}(m,l,s,u,t)\text{ holds for }\{(m,l):l=k\text{ and }m\leq n-1,\text{ or }l=k-1,m\leq n+2\}\implies{\cal S}(n,k,s,u,t)\text{ holds}.

Suppose the last statement holds. We now prove (
2.77
).

Before delving into the detailed proof, we note that a heuristic argument will be presented after the proof. This heuristic provides a high-level, intuitive explanation of the underlying ideas and complements the rigorous derivation offered here. Readers are encouraged to refer to these paragraphs, either before or after reading the detailed proof, for additional insights.

Proof of (
2.77
).

With the bound (
2.73
) for
\cal L
and (
3.46
) for
\cal K
,

{\cal S}(m,l,s,u,t)
holds for
l=0
and any
m\geq 1
.
By (
2.76
),

{\cal S}(m,l,s,u,t)
holds for any
l
and
m\leq 2
. By (
5.109
),
{\cal S}(m,l,s,u,t)
holds for
(3,1)
.
Then we apply (
5.109
) again and
{\cal S}(m,l,s,u,t)
holds for
(4,1)
. We can continue this procedure until

{\cal S}(m,l,s,u,t)
holds for
(n,1)
for any
n
. We can now repeat this process and prove that
{\cal S}(m,l,s,u,t)
holds for
(n,2)
for any
n
. Eventually, the induction implies that
{\cal S}(n,k,s,u,t)
for any
n,k
.
By condition (
2.72
), for any fixed
n
there is a large enough
k
so that

(\ell_{t}/\ell_{s})^{n-1}(W\ell_{s}\eta_{s})^{1-k/4}\ll 1.

Thus the fact that
{\cal S}(n,k,s,u,t)
holds for such
n,k
implies that

\Xi^{({\cal L-K})}_{u,n}\prec(W\ell_{s}\eta_{s})^{1/2}
. Together with (
5.107
), we have proved (
2.77
).

Now we only need to prove (
5.109
). Recall
at the time
s
that the following initial bound holds:

(5.110)

\Xi_{s,n}^{(\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K})}\prec 1.

We claim that

(5.111)

\displaystyle\Xi^{({\cal L})}_{u,2n+2}\prec\Psi(n,k,s,u,t)^{2}.

Using this bound as an input, from (
5.92
) we obtain that

(5.112)

\displaystyle\Xi_{t^{\prime},\,n}^{(\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K})}\prec\;

\displaystyle\;\max_{v\in[s,t^{\prime}]}\left(\Psi(n,k,s,v,t^{\prime})+\;\max_{m<n}\Xi^{({\cal L-\cal K})}_{v,m}+\max_{m:\;2\leq m\leq n}\Xi^{({\cal L-\cal K})}_{v,m}\cdot\Xi^{({\cal L-\cal K})}_{v,n-m+2}\cdot(W\ell_{v}\eta_{v})^{-1}+\Xi^{({\cal L})}_{v,n+1}\right)

for any
s\leq t^{\prime}\leq t
. We aim to prove the conclusion of (
5.109
) for a fixed
(n,k)
. Using (
5.107
), the last term
\Xi^{({\cal L})}_{v,n+1}
can be bounded by
\Xi^{{(\cal L-\cal K})}_{v,n+1}
with a small prefactor.
Inserting this bound and the assumption (
5.109
) into the last display, we have

(5.113)

\displaystyle\Xi_{t^{\prime},\,n}^{(\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K})}\;\prec\;\max_{v\in[s,t^{\prime}]}\Psi(n,k,s,v,t^{\prime}).

Here we have used
k\geq 1
. Setting
t^{\prime}=u
and using the monotonicity of
\Psi
in the
t
variable, we have proved the conclusion of (
5.109
).

We now prove (
5.111
) for
\Xi^{(\cal L)}_{u,{2n+2}}
under the assumption in (
5.109
). First, the long loop can be bounded with short chains. Let

\boldsymbol{\sigma}=(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\ldots,\sigma_{n}),\quad\textbf{a}=(a_{1},a_{2},\ldots,a_{n-1}),\quad\sigma_{k}\in\{+,-\},\quad a_{k}\in\mathbb{Z}_{L}.

We temporally define a
G
-chain as

(5.114)

{\cal C}_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}=G_{t}(\sigma_{1})E_{a_{1}}G_{t}(\sigma_{2})E_{a_{2}}\cdots E_{a_{n-1}}G_{t}(\sigma_{n}).

Given an arbitrary rank
(2n+2)
loop
{\cal L}^{(2n+2)}_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma},{\bf{a}}}
with

\boldsymbol{\sigma}=(\sigma,\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{n},\sigma^{\prime},\sigma_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,\sigma_{n}^{\prime}),\quad{\bf{a}}=(b,a_{1},\ldots,a_{n},b^{\prime},a_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,a_{n}^{\prime}),

we can write it is

\displaystyle{\cal L}^{(2n+2)}_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma},{\bf{a}}}=W^{-2d}\sum_{i\in{\cal I}_{b},\,j\in{\cal I}_{b^{\prime}}}\left({\cal C}^{(n+1)}_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{1},{\bf{a}}_{1}}\right)_{ij}\left({\cal C}^{(n+1)}_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{2},{\bf{a}}_{2}}\right)_{ji},

where
\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{1}=(\sigma,\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{n},\sigma^{\prime})
,
\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{2}=(\sigma_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,\sigma_{n}^{\prime},\sigma)
,
{\bf{a}}_{1}=(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n})
, and
{\bf{a}}_{2}=(a_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,a_{n}^{\prime})
. Applying the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we obtain that

(5.115)

\max_{{\bf{a}}}\max_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}\in\{+,-\}^{2n+2}}\left|{\cal L}^{(2n+2)}_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma},{\bf{a}}}\right|\leq\max_{{\bf{a}}_{1}}\max_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{1}\in\{+,-\}^{n+1}}W^{-2}\sum_{i\in{\cal I}_{b},\,j\in{\cal I}_{b^{\prime}}}\left|\left({\cal C}^{(n+1)}_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{1},{\bf{a}}_{1}}\right)_{ij}\right|^{2}.

We now split the
(n+1)
chain further into an
l_{1}
-
G
chain and an
l_{2}
-
G
chain, where
l_{1}=\lfloor(n+1)/{2}\rfloor
and
l_{2}=n+1-l_{1}
.
Using the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality again, we get that

(5.116)

\displaystyle W^{-2}\sum_{i\in{\cal I}_{b},\,j\in{\cal I}_{b^{\prime}}}\left|\left({\cal C}^{(n+1)}_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{1},{\bf{a}}_{1}}\right)_{ij}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq W^{-2}\sum_{i\in{\cal I}_{b},\,j\in{\cal I}_{b^{\prime}}}\left({\cal C}^{(2l_{1})}_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{1}^{\prime},{\bf{a}}_{1}^{\prime}}\right)_{ii}\left({\cal C}^{(2l_{2})}_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{2}^{\prime},{\bf{a}}_{2}^{\prime}}\right)_{jj}\;=\;{\cal L}^{(2l_{1})}_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{1}^{\prime},\widetilde{\bf{a}}_{1}}\cdot{\cal L}^{(2l_{2})}_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{2}^{\prime},\widetilde{\bf{a}}_{2}},

where
\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{1}^{\prime}
,
{\bf{a}}_{1}^{\prime}
,
\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{2}^{\prime}
,
{\bf{a}}_{2}^{\prime}
are defined by

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{1}^{\prime}=(\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{l_{1}},-\sigma_{l_{1}},\ldots,-\sigma_{1}),\quad

\displaystyle{\bf{a}}_{1}^{\prime}=(a_{1},\ldots,a_{l_{1}-1},a_{l_{1}},a_{l_{1}-1},\ldots,a_{1}),

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{2}^{\prime}=(-\sigma_{n+1},\ldots,-\sigma_{l_{1}+1},\sigma_{l_{1}+1},\ldots,\sigma_{n+1}),\quad

\displaystyle{\bf{a}}_{2}^{\prime}=(a_{n},\ldots,a_{l_{1}+1},a_{l_{1}},a_{l_{1}+1},\ldots,a_{n}),

\widetilde{\bf{a}}_{1}
is obtained by adding
b
to
{\bf{a}}_{1}^{\prime}
, and
\widetilde{\bf{a}}_{2}
is obtained by adding
b
to
{\bf{a}}_{2}^{\prime}
. Plugging (
5.116
) into (
5.115
) and using that
l_{1}+l_{2}=n+1
, we obtain

(5.117)

\displaystyle\max_{{\bf{a}}}\max_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}\in\{+,-\}^{2n+2}}\left|{\cal L}^{(2n+2)}_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma},{\bf{a}}}\right|\leq\left(\max_{{\bf{a}}}\max_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}\in\{+,-\}^{2l_{1}}}\left|{\cal L}^{(2l_{1})}_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma},{\bf{a}}}\right|\right)\cdot\left(\max_{{\bf{a}}}\max_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}\in\{+,-\}^{2l_{2}}}\left|{\cal L}^{(2l_{2})}_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma},{\bf{a}}}\right|\right),

i.e.

(5.118)

\displaystyle\Xi^{({\cal L})}_{u,2n+2}\leq\Xi^{({\cal L})}_{u,2l_{1}}\cdot\Xi^{({\cal L})}_{u,2l_{2}}\cdot(W\ell_{u}\eta_{u}).

Since
\max(2l_{1},2l_{2})\leq n+2
, with the assumption of (
5.109
) and (
5.118
), we can bound
\Xi^{({\cal L})}_{u,2n+2}
as follows

(5.119)

\displaystyle\Xi^{({\cal L})}_{u,2n+2}\;\prec\;

\displaystyle(W\ell_{u}\eta_{u})\cdot\prod_{m=1}^{2}\left(1+(W\ell_{u}\eta_{u})^{-1}\cdot\Psi(2l_{1},k-1,s,u,t)\right).

Then with
l_{1}+l_{2}=n+1
, and
|l_{1}-l_{2}|\leq 1
, we obtain that

(5.120)

\displaystyle\Xi^{({\cal L})}_{u,2n+2}\;\prec\;

\displaystyle\left((W\ell_{s}\eta_{s})^{1/2}+(\ell_{t}/\ell_{s})^{n-1}(W\ell_{s}\eta_{s})^{1-k/4}\right)^{2}\;=\;

\displaystyle\Psi(n,k,s,u,t)^{2}.

Hence we have proved (
5.111
). This completes the proof of (
2.77
), i.e., the Step 3 of proving Theorem
2.21
.

∎

Roughly speaking, an
n-{\cal L}_{u}
loop is of order
A_{u}^{-n+1}
with
A_{u}\sim W\ell_{u}\eta_{u}
.
Assuming
n
is odd for simplicity, from (
5.118
) we have that

(5.121)

\Xi^{({\cal L})}_{u,2n+2}\leq\big(\Xi^{({\cal L})}_{u,n+1}\big)^{2}A_{u}

Hence (
5.92
) takes the form

(5.122)

\displaystyle\Xi_{t^{\prime},\,n}^{(\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K})}\prec\;\;\max_{v\in[s,t^{\prime}]}\left(\Xi^{({\cal L})}_{v,n+1}A_{v}^{1/2}+\;\max_{m<n}\Xi^{({\cal L-\cal K})}_{v,m}+\max_{m:\;2\leq m\leq n}\Xi^{({\cal L-\cal K})}_{v,m}\cdot\Xi^{({\cal L-\cal K})}_{v,n-m+2}A_{v}^{-1}+\Xi^{({\cal L})}_{v,n+1}\right)

The third term on the right hand side is of lower order while the second term is bounded by induction on
n
. We are left with the first and the last terms which involving
n+1
(and
n+2
in case
n
is even)
\cal L
loops. By (
5.107
),

(5.123)

\displaystyle\Xi^{(\cal L)}_{v,n+1}\prec 1+A_{v}^{-1}\Xi^{({\cal L-\cal K})}_{v,n+1}.

Therefore, up to terms which are either negligible or can be bounded by induction, we have

(5.124)

\displaystyle\Xi_{t^{\prime},\,n}^{(\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K})}\prec\;\;\max_{v\in[s,t^{\prime}]}\left(A_{v}^{1/2}+A_{v}^{-1/2}\Xi^{({\cal L-\cal K})}_{v,n+1}+A_{v}^{-1}\Xi^{({\cal L-\cal K})}_{v,n+1}\right)

Although we cannot bound
\Xi_{n}^{(\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K})}
by
\Xi_{n+1}^{(\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K})}
by induction,
we can derive rough bounds on
\Xi_{n}^{(\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K})}
for all
n
and then bootstrap the argument. This is basically our procedure to arrive at
\Xi_{t,\,n}^{(\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K})}\prec\;A_{t}^{1/2}
.

5.7
Proof of Theorem
2.21
, step 4 and 5.

Step 4: Proof of (
2.78
) .

Recall the key inequalities (
5.111
) and (
5.112
) in the proof of step 3.
We can now use (
2.77
) to bound the
\Xi^{(\cal L)}_{2n+2}
in (
5.111
) and
\Xi^{(\cal L)}_{n+1}
in (
5.112
). Then we obtain

(5.125)

\displaystyle\Xi_{t,\,n}^{(\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K})}\prec\;

\displaystyle\;1+\max_{u\in[s,t]}\left(\;\max_{k<n}\Xi^{({\cal L-\cal K})}_{u,k}+\max_{k:\;2\leq k\leq n}\Xi^{({\cal L-\cal K})}_{u,k}\cdot\Xi^{({\cal L-\cal K})}_{u,n-k+2}\cdot(W\ell_{u}\eta_{u})^{-1}\right).

By (
2.76
) and (
4.5
) for
(\cal L-\cal K)
-loops or length
1
and
2
and
the condition (
2.72
), we have

\Xi_{t,\,1}^{(\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K})}\prec 1,\quad\Xi_{t,\,2}^{(\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K})}\prec(W\ell_{u}\eta_{u})^{1/4}.

Using (
5.125
) and induction on
n
, one can easily prove

\Xi_{t,\,n}^{(\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K})}\prec 1
for any fixed
n
. This completes the proof of (
2.78
).
Notice that the prefactor
(\eta_{s}/\eta_{t})^{2}
in Step 2 was eliminated in Lemma
5.14
partly by using the sum zero property.
It is important that this factor is eliminated so it will not accumulate from the time splitting argument in the proof of Theorem
2.21
.
∎

Step 5: Proof of (
2.79
).

Our goal is to prove
{\cal J}^{*}_{t,D}\prec 1
. To this end, note that
we have proved in Step 4 that

\left({\cal L}-{\cal K}\right)_{t,\,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\,\textbf{a}}\prec(W\ell_{t}\eta_{t})^{-2}

for the
{\cal L}-{\cal K}
-loop of length
2
. It implies that

\left({\cal L}-{\cal K}\right)_{t,\,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\,\textbf{a}}\Big/{\cal T}_{t,D}(|a_{1}-a_{2}|)\prec 1,\quad\text{ for }|a_{1}-a_{2}|=O(\ell_{t}^{*}).

It remains to consider the case
|a_{1}-a_{2}|>6\ell_{t}^{*}
. But (
2.79
) is a consequence of (
5.48
) in this case.
This completes the proof of Step 5.

∎

5.8
Proof of Theorem
2.21
, step 6

In this step, we have the estimates in the assumption (
2.71
) and all results from step 4 and 5, i.e., (
2.78
) and (
2.79
). Our goal is to prove (
2.80
).
We first prove the following bound on
\mathbb{E}{(\cal L-\cal K)}
which improves the
1
loop bound on
{(\cal L-\cal K)}
.

Lemma 5.15
.

(5.126)

\displaystyle\max_{a}\left|\mathbb{E}({\cal L-\cal K})_{u,\;(+),\;(a)}\right|=\max_{a}\left|\mathbb{E}\langle(G_{u}-m)E_{a}\rangle\right|\prec(W\ell_{u}\eta_{u})^{-2}.

Proof of Lemma
5.15
.

We will denote
G_{u}
by
G
in this proof.
Recall that
G-m=-m(H+m)G
. Using Gaussian integration by parts, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\langle(G-m)E_{a}\rangle

\displaystyle=m\mathbb{E}\langle(-H-m)GE_{a}\rangle

\displaystyle=um\sum_{b}\mathbb{E}\langle(G-m)E_{b}\rangle W\cdot S^{(B)}_{bb^{\prime}}\langle E_{b^{\prime}}GE_{a}\rangle

(5.127)

\displaystyle=um\sum_{b}\mathbb{E}\langle(G-m)E_{b}\rangle S^{(B)}_{ba}\langle GE_{a}\rangle.

Writing
\langle GE_{a}\rangle=m+\langle(G-m)E_{a}\rangle
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\langle(G-m)E_{a}\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{a^{\prime}}\left((1-um^{2}S^{(B)})^{-1}\right)_{aa^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}\left(um\sum_{b}\langle(G-m)E_{b}\rangle S^{(B)}_{ba^{\prime}}\langle(G-m)E_{a^{\prime}}\rangle\right)

(5.128)

\displaystyle=\sum_{a^{\prime}}\left(\Theta^{(B)}_{um^{2}}\right)_{aa^{\prime}}\cdot\mathbb{E}\left(um\sum_{b}\langle(G-m)E_{b}\rangle S^{(B)}_{ba^{\prime}}\langle(G-m)E_{a^{\prime}}\rangle\right).

From (
2.78
), we know that
\langle(G-m)E_{a^{\prime}}\rangle\prec(W\ell_{u}\eta_{u})^{-1}
. Substituting this into the above estimate, we obtain (
5.126
) and have completed the proof of Lemma
5.15
.
∎

Proof of (
2.80
).

Taking expectation of both side of (
5.3
) for the case
n=2
, we have

(5.129)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\;(\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K})_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\;=\;\;

\displaystyle\left(\mathcal{U}_{s,t,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\circ\mathbb{E}\,(\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K})_{s,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\right)_{\textbf{a}}

(5.130)

\displaystyle+\;\int_{s}^{t}\left(\mathcal{U}_{u,t,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\circ\mathbb{E}\,\mathcal{E}^{((\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K})\times(\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K}))}_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\right)_{\textbf{a}}du

(5.131)

\displaystyle+\int_{s}^{t}\left(\mathcal{U}_{u,t,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\circ\mathbb{E}\,\mathcal{E}^{(\widetilde{G})}_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\right)_{\textbf{a}}du.

By assumption, we have

(5.132)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\;(\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K})_{s,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\prec(W\ell_{s}\eta_{s})^{-3}.

Similarly
{\cal E}^{((L-K)\times(L-K))}_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}
can be bounded as the product of two
{\cal(L-K)}
, and with the fast decay property, we obtain

(5.133)

\displaystyle{\cal E}^{((L-K)\times(L-K))}_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\prec W\ell_{u}(W\ell_{u}\eta_{u})^{-4}\prec(\eta_{u})^{-1}(W\ell_{u}\eta_{u})^{-3}

Similarly to the arguments used in proving (
5.52
), we can bound
\mathbb{E}\mathcal{E}^{(\widetilde{G})}
as the product of a

1
-
{\cal(L-K)}
loop and a
3
-
\cal L
loop. Writing
{\cal L}={\cal K}+({\cal L}-{\cal K})
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\mathcal{E}^{(\widetilde{G})}_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\;\prec\;

\displaystyle\;W\cdot\ell_{u}\cdot\max_{a}\max_{\textbf{a}}\max_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}\in\{+,-\}^{3}}\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[\langle(G-M)E_{a}\rangle\cdot{\cal L}_{u,\textbf{a},\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\Big]\Big|

\displaystyle\;\prec\;

\displaystyle\;W\cdot\ell_{u}\cdot\max_{a}\big|\mathbb{E}\langle(G-M)E_{a}\rangle\big|\cdot\max_{\textbf{a}}\max_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}\in\{+,-\}^{3}}{\cal K}_{u,\textbf{a},\boldsymbol{\sigma}}

(5.134)

\displaystyle+\;\;

\displaystyle W\cdot\ell_{u}\cdot\max_{a}\big|{({\cal L-\cal K})}_{u,(+),(a),}\rangle\big|\cdot\max_{\textbf{a}}\max_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}\in\{+,-\}^{3}}{({\cal L-\cal K})}_{u,\textbf{a},\boldsymbol{\sigma}}.

Using (
5.126
) for
\mathbb{E}\langle(G-M)E_{a}\rangle
, (
2.59
) for
\cal K
,
and (
2.78
) for
\cal L-\cal K
, we have

(5.135)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\mathcal{E}^{(\widetilde{G})}_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\prec\;\;

\displaystyle(\eta_{u})^{-1}(W\ell_{u}\eta_{u})^{-3}.

One can easily see that all tensors discussed above have fast decay. Applying (
7.14
) to bound
\max\to\max
norm of
{\cal U}_{u,t}
and using (
5.132
), (
5.133
) and (
5.135
), we can estimate the right hand side of (
5.129
) by

(5.136)

\displaystyle(W\ell_{t}\eta_{t})^{3}\cdot\mathbb{E}\;(\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K})_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\;\prec(\ell_{t}/\ell_{s})\frac{\ell_{t}\eta_{t}}{\ell_{s}\eta_{s}}+\int_{s}^{t}(\ell_{t}/\ell_{u})\frac{\ell_{t}\eta_{t}}{\ell_{u}\eta_{u}}\cdot\eta_{u}^{-1}du\prec 1,

where we have used

u\leq t\implies\frac{\ell^{2}_{t}\eta_{t}}{\ell^{2}_{u}\eta_{u}}\leq 1.

This completes the proof of (
2.80
).
∎

6
Continuity Estimates on Loops

In this section, we prove Lemma
5.1
. Recall
t_{1}\leq t_{2}\leq 1,\;G_{t_{1}}=(H_{t_{1}}-z_{t_{1}})^{-1}
and
G_{t_{2}}=(H_{t_{2}}-z_{t_{2}})^{-1}
.
In this section, we will use the notation

\widetilde{G}_{t_{1}}:=\left(H_{t_{2}}-\widetilde{z}_{t_{1}}\right)^{-1},\quad\widetilde{z}_{t_{1}}:=\left(\frac{t_{2}}{t_{1}}\right)^{1/2}z_{t_{1}}

and denote by
\widetilde{\cal L}_{t_{1}}
the loops with resolvent
\widetilde{G}_{t_{1}}
.
By scaling, we have

(6.1)

\widetilde{\cal L}_{t_{1}}\sim\left(\frac{t_{1}}{t_{2}}\right)^{n/2}{\cal L}_{t_{1}},\quad\widetilde{G}_{t_{1}}\sim\left(\frac{t_{1}}{t_{2}}\right)^{1/2}\cdot{G}_{t_{1}}\quad\hbox{in distribution}.

One can check that
|z_{t_{2}}-\widetilde{z}_{t_{1}}|\leq C(1-t_{1})
for
t_{1}>c>0
. From now on, we drop the
t
subscript
and use the following notations

(6.2)

\displaystyle G_{t_{2}}\longleftrightarrow G,\quad\widetilde{G}_{t_{1}}\longleftrightarrow\widetilde{G},\quad\quad{\cal L}_{t_{2}}\longleftrightarrow{\cal L},\quad\widetilde{{\cal L}}_{t_{1}}\longleftrightarrow\widetilde{{\cal L}},\quad\quad{z}_{t_{2}}\longleftrightarrow{z},\quad\widetilde{{z}}_{t_{1}}\longleftrightarrow\widetilde{{z}}.

With these notations and the resolvent formula,

(6.3)

G=\widetilde{G}+(z-\widetilde{z})\cdot G\cdot\widetilde{G}.

We first recall a basic linear algebra fact.

Lemma 6.1
.

Let
v
and
w_{i}
(
1\leq i\leq m
) be vectors in a Hilbert space
H
and
A_{ij}=(w_{i},w_{j})
.
Then for any
p\geq 1
,

\sum_{i}|(v,w_{i})|^{2}\leq\|v\|_{2}^{2}\cdot(\operatorname{tr}A^{p})^{1/p}.

Proof.

Define a linear operator
T:H\to\mathbb{C}^{m}
by
w\to\sum_{i}(w,w_{i})e_{i}
. Then

\frac{\sum_{i}|(v,w_{i})|^{2}}{\|v\|_{2}}=\frac{\|Tv\|^{2}}{\|v\|^{2}}=\|T^{*}T\|_{l_{2}\to l_{2}}=\|TT^{*}\|_{l_{2}\to l_{2}}=\|A\|_{l_{2}\to l_{2}}\leq(\operatorname{tr}A^{p})^{1/p}.

∎

Proof of Lemma
5.1
.

Using the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we know that the
G
loop with length
2m+1
(
m\geq 1
) can be bounded by the
G
loop with lengths
2m
and
2m+2
, i.e.,

(6.4)

\displaystyle\max_{\textbf{a},\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\left|\mathcal{L}^{(\text{length}=2m+1)}_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\right|^{2}\leq\left|\max_{\textbf{a},\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\mathcal{L}^{(\text{length}=2m)}_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\cdot\max_{\textbf{a},\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\mathcal{L}^{(\text{length}=2m+2)}_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\right|.

Therefore, we will only prove (
5.7
) for even loop lengths. Using the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality again, we can assume that the loop is symmetric (recall
G
chain
\cal C
defined in (
5.114
)), i.e.,

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{\prime},\textbf{a}^{\prime}}=

\displaystyle\langle E_{a_{0}}\cdot\mathcal{C}_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\cdot E_{a_{m}}\cdot\mathcal{C}^{\dagger}_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\rangle,\quad\boldsymbol{\sigma}=(z_{1},z_{2},\ldots,z_{m}),\quad\textbf{a}=(a_{1},a_{2},\ldots,a_{m-1})

(6.5)

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{\prime}=

\displaystyle(z_{1},z_{2},\ldots,z_{m},\overline{z}_{m},\overline{z}_{m-1},\ldots,\overline{z}_{1}),\quad\textbf{a}^{\prime}=(a_{1},a_{2},\ldots,a_{m-1},a_{m},a_{m-1},\ldots,a_{1},a_{0}).

We only need to prove that for any fixed
m\geq 1
, the following holds

(6.6)

{\bf 1}_{\Omega}\cdot\mathcal{L}_{t_{2},\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{\prime},\textbf{a}^{\prime}}\prec\left(W\cdot\ell_{t_{1}}\cdot\eta_{t_{2}}\right)^{-2m+1},

under the inductive assumption that this bound holds for
m^{\prime}\leq m-1
for all
a
and
\boldsymbol{\sigma}
.
Recall the identity

(6.7)

\prod_{k=1}^{m}(a_{k}+b_{k})=\prod_{k=1}^{m}a_{k}+\sum_{l=1}^{m}\left(\prod_{j=1}^{l-1}(a_{j}+b_{j})\right)b_{l}\left(\prod_{j=l+1}^{m}a_{j}\right).

With the notations
G_{i}=G_{t_{2}}(\sigma_{i}),\quad\widetilde{G}_{i}={\widetilde{G}}_{t_{1}}(\sigma_{i})
,
we apply this identity with
a_{i}+b_{i}\to G_{i},\quad a\to\widetilde{G}_{i}
.
Together with the resolvent identity (
6.3
), we have

\displaystyle G_{1}E_{a_{1}}\cdots G_{m-1}E_{a_{m-1}}G_{m}=

\displaystyle\;\widetilde{G}_{1}E_{a_{1}}\cdots\widetilde{G}_{m-1}E_{a_{m-1}}\widetilde{G}_{m}

(6.8)

\displaystyle+(z-\widetilde{z})\cdot\sum_{l=1}^{m}G_{1}E_{a_{1}}\cdots G_{l-1}E_{a_{l-1}}\cdot\left(G_{l}\cdot\widetilde{G}_{l}\right)E_{a_{l}}\widetilde{G}_{l+1}\cdots\widetilde{G}_{m}.

In the last term, there are
l
instances of
G
and
m-l+1
instances of
\widetilde{G}

and there is no matrix
E
between
G_{l}
and
\widetilde{G}_{l}
.
It implies that for any
i
and
j
,

\displaystyle\left|\left(G_{1}E_{a_{1}}\cdots G_{m-1}E_{a_{m-1}}G_{m}\right)_{ij}\right|^{2}\leq C_{m}

\displaystyle\;\left|\left(\widetilde{G}_{1}E_{a_{1}}\cdots\widetilde{G}_{m-1}E_{a_{m-1}}\widetilde{G}_{m}\right)_{ij}\right|^{2}

(6.9)

\displaystyle+C_{m}\left|z_{t_{2}}-\widetilde{z}_{t_{1}}\right|^{2}\cdot\sum_{l=1}^{m}\left|\left(G_{1}E_{a_{1}}\cdots G_{l}\cdot\widetilde{G}_{l}E_{a_{l}}\cdots\widetilde{G}_{m}\right)_{ij}\right|^{2}.

For any
l,j
, denote by
v^{(l)}\in\mathbb{C}^{N},\;w^{(l)}_{j}\in\mathbb{C}^{N}
the vectors with components given by

v^{(l)}(k)=\left(G_{1}E_{a_{1}}\cdots E_{a_{l-1}}G_{l}\right)_{ik},\quad\overline{w^{(l)}_{j}(k)}=\left(\widetilde{G}_{l}E_{a_{l}}\cdots E_{a_{m-1}}\widetilde{G}_{m}\right)_{kj}.

Here
i
is treated as a given index and it will not play any active role in the following proof. Denote by
A^{(l)}
the matrix with elements given by

A^{(l)}_{jj^{\prime}}=\left(w^{(l)}_{j},w^{(l)}_{j^{\prime}}\right),\quad j,j^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}_{a_{m}}.

Applying Lemma
6.1
to the last term in (
6
), we have

(6.10)

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in\mathcal{I}_{a_{m}}}\left|\left(G_{1}E_{a_{1}}\cdots G_{l}\cdot\widetilde{G}_{l}E_{a_{l}}\cdots\widetilde{G}_{m}\right)_{ij}\right|^{2}=\sum_{j\in\mathcal{I}_{a_{m}}}\left|\left(v^{(l)},w^{(l)}_{j}\right)\right|^{2}\leq\|v^{(l)}\|_{2}^{2}\left(\operatorname{tr}\left(A^{(l)}\right)^{p}\right)^{1/p}.

With the identity
\widetilde{G}\cdot\widetilde{G}^{\dagger}=\frac{\widetilde{G}-\widetilde{G}^{\dagger}}{2\operatorname{Im}\widetilde{z}}

A^{(l)}_{jj^{\prime}}
can be written as
\widetilde{\cal C}
chain of length
2m-2l+1
, namely,

A^{(l)}_{jj^{\prime}}=\frac{1}{2\operatorname{Im}\widetilde{z}}\left[\left(\widetilde{G}_{m}E_{a_{m-1}}\cdots E_{a_{l}}\widetilde{G}_{l}\overline{E_{a_{l}}\cdots E_{a_{m-1}}\widetilde{G}_{m}}\right)_{j^{\prime}j}-\left(\widetilde{G}_{m}E_{a_{m-1}}\cdots E_{a_{l}}\overline{\widetilde{G}_{l}E_{a_{l}}\cdots E_{a_{m-1}}\widetilde{G}_{m}}\right)_{j^{\prime}j}\right].

Hence we can write
\left(\frac{2\operatorname{Im}\widetilde{z}}{W}\right)^{p}\cdot\operatorname{tr}\left(A^{(l)}\right)^{p}

as a sum of
2^{p}

\widetilde{\cal L}_{t_{1}}-
loops of Using
2p(m-l)+p
. With (
6.1
), we can bound

\widetilde{\cal L}_{t_{1}}
with
{\cal L}_{t_{1}}
. Using the inductive assumption on
{\cal L}_{t_{1}}
, we have

\left(\operatorname{tr}\left(A^{(l)}\right)^{p}\right)^{1/p}\prec\frac{W}{\operatorname{Im}\widetilde{z}}\left(W\ell_{t_{1}}\eta_{t_{1}}\right)^{-2(m-l)-1+1/p},\quad\im\widetilde{z}_{t_{1}}\sim\im z_{t_{1}}.

Inserting this bound into (
6.10
) and (
6
) and averaging over
i\in\mathcal{I}_{a_{0}}
and
j\in\mathcal{I}_{a_{m}}
, we can bound (
6
) by

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{t_{2},\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{\prime},\textbf{a}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\;=\left\langle E_{a_{0}}\cdot\mathcal{C}_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\cdot E_{a_{m}}\cdot\mathcal{C}^{\dagger}_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\right\rangle

(6.11)
