\displaystyle\;\prec\;\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{t_{1},\;\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{\prime},\textbf{a}^{\prime}}+\frac{|z_{t_{2}}-\widetilde{z}_{t_{1}}|^{2}}{W\operatorname{Im}\widetilde{z}_{t_{1}}}\sum_{l=1}^{m}\left(\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{a_{0}}}\|v^{(l)}\|_{2}^{2}\right)\cdot\left(W\ell_{t_{1}}\eta_{t_{1}}\right)^{-2(m-l)-1}.

Here we have taken
p
to be large enough so that
1/p
can be absorbed dropped in the
\prec
notation.
By definition, one can easily check that (here we use the assumption that
t_{1}>c>0
for some
c
)

|z_{t_{2}}-\widetilde{z}_{t_{1}}|^{2}\leq C|t_{2}-t_{1}|^{2}\leq C\left(\eta_{t_{1}}\right)^{2},\quad\frac{|z_{t_{2}}-\widetilde{z}_{t_{1}}|^{2}}{\operatorname{Im}\widetilde{z}_{t_{1}}}\leq C\eta_{t_{1}}.

Using Ward’s identity
G\cdot G^{\dagger}=\frac{G-G^{\dagger}}{2\operatorname{Im}z}
again, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{W}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{a_{0}}}\|v^{(l)}\|_{2}^{2}=\frac{1}{2\operatorname{Im}z}\left(\mathcal{L}_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{l}^{(1)},\textbf{a}_{l}}-\mathcal{L}_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{l}^{(2)},\textbf{a}_{l}}\right),\quad\textbf{a}_{l}=(a_{0},a_{1},\ldots,a_{l-1},a_{l-1},\ldots,a_{1}),

(6.12)

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{l}^{(1)}=(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\ldots,\sigma_{l-1},\sigma_{l},\overline{\sigma}_{l-1},\ldots,\overline{\sigma}_{2},\overline{\sigma}_{1}),\quad\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{l}^{(2)}=(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\ldots,\sigma_{l-1},\overline{\sigma}_{l},\overline{\sigma}_{l-1},\ldots,\overline{\sigma}_{2},\overline{\sigma}_{1}).

Here the
\cal L
loops are length
2l-1
.
We can bound these loops by induction so that

{\bf 1}_{\Omega}\cdot\frac{1}{W}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{a_{0}}}\|v^{(l)}\|_{2}^{2}\prec\frac{1}{\operatorname{Im}z}\left(W\ell_{t_{1}}\eta_{t_{2}}\right)^{-2l+2},\quad l<m,\quad z=z_{t_{2}}.

Inserting this bound to (
6
), we have proved that

(6.13)

{\bf 1}_{\Omega}\cdot\mathcal{L}_{t_{2},\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{\prime},\textbf{a}^{\prime}}\prec\left(W\ell_{t_{1}}\eta_{t_{2}}\right)^{-2m+1}+\frac{\eta_{t_{1}}}{\eta_{t_{2}}}\cdot\left(\mathcal{L}_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{m}^{(1)},\textbf{a}_{m}}-\mathcal{L}_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{m}^{(2)},\textbf{a}_{m}}\right)\left(W\ell_{t_{1}}\eta_{t_{1}}\right)^{-1}.

Here
\mathcal{L}_{t_{2},\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{m}^{(1)},\textbf{a}_{m}}
and
\mathcal{L}_{t_{2},\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{m}^{(2)},\textbf{a}_{m}}
are
G
loops with the length
2m-1
and
\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{m}^{(1)},\;\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{m}^{(2)}\in\{+,-\}^{2m-1}
.

For
m=1
, by definition (
5.6
),

{\bf 1}_{\Omega}\cdot\mathcal{L}_{t_{2},\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{m}^{(1)},\textbf{a}_{m}},\;\;{\bf 1}_{\Omega}\cdot\mathcal{L}_{t_{2},\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{m}^{(2)},\textbf{a}_{m}}\prec 1,\quad m=1,\quad\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{m}^{(1)},\;\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{m}^{(2)}\in\{+,-\}.

Inserting these bounds to (
6.13
), we obtain (
6.6
) in the case
m=1
.
For
m>1
, applying (
6.4
) again, we can bound
2m-1
-
G
-loop by a
G-
loop of length
2m-2
and a
G-
loop of length
2m
. Furthermore, we can use assumption (
6.6
) to bound this
G
loop with length
2m-2
. Then we obtain that

{\bf 1}_{\Omega}\cdot\mathcal{L}_{t_{2},\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{m}^{(1,2)},\textbf{a}_{m}}\prec\left[\left(W\ell_{t_{1}}\eta_{t_{2}}\right)^{-2m+3}\max_{\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\left|\mathcal{L}^{\text{length=2m}}_{t_{2},\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\right|\right]^{1/2}.

Inserting this bound to (
6.13
), we obtain

{\bf 1}_{\Omega}\cdot\max_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{\prime},\textbf{a}^{\prime}}\left|\mathcal{L}^{\text{length=2m}}_{t_{2},\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{\prime},\textbf{a}^{\prime}}\right|\prec\left(W\ell_{t_{1}}\eta_{t_{2}}\right)^{-2m+1}+\left(W\ell_{t_{1}}\eta_{t_{2}}\right)^{-m+1/2}\max_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{\prime},\textbf{a}^{\prime}}\left|\mathcal{L}^{\text{length=2m}}_{t_{2},\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{\prime},\textbf{a}^{\prime}}\right|^{1/2}.

This implies (
6.6
) and we have thus proved Lemma
5.1
.
∎

7
Auxiliary proofs and technical estimates

7.1
Evolution kernel estimates

We first prove a simple
L_{\max\to\max}
bound for
\cal U

5.17
in the following lemma.

Lemma 7.1

(
\|{\cal U}\|_{\max\to\max}
estimate)
.

Let
\cal A
be a tensor
\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{n}\to\mathbb{C}
and
n\geq 2
.
Then

(7.1)

\displaystyle\|{\cal U}_{s,t,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\;\circ{\cal A}\|_{\max}\prec\|{\cal A}\|_{\max}\cdot\left(\eta_{s}/\eta_{t}\right)^{n},\quad s<t.

Proof.

Recall that
{\cal U}_{s,t}
was defined in
5.17
. We write

\left({\cal U}_{s,t}\circ{\cal A}^{\cal T}\right)_{\textbf{a}}=\sum_{\textbf{b}}\prod_{i=1}^{2}(\Theta_{t}\Theta^{-1}_{s})_{a_{i}b_{i}}{\cal A}_{\textbf{b}}.

With
\Theta_{t}\cdot\Theta_{s}^{-1}=1-(t-s)\cdot S^{(B)}\cdot\Theta_{t}
, we have

\displaystyle\prod_{i=1}^{2}(\Theta_{t}\Theta^{-1}_{s})_{a_{i}b_{i}}=

\displaystyle\prod_{i=1}^{2}\delta_{a_{i}b_{i}}-(t-s)\delta_{a_{1}b_{1}}\cdot\left(S^{(B)}\cdot\Theta_{t}\right)_{a_{2}b_{2}}-(t-s)\delta_{a_{2}b_{2}}\cdot\left(S^{(B)}\cdot\Theta_{t}\right)_{a_{1}b_{1}}

(7.2)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle(t-s)^{2}\left(S^{(B)}\cdot\Theta_{t}\right)_{a_{1}b_{1}}\left(S^{(B)}\cdot\Theta_{t}\right)_{a_{2}b_{2}}:={\cal V}^{(1)}_{\textbf{a},\textbf{b}}+{\cal V}^{(2)}_{\textbf{a},\textbf{b}}+{\cal V}^{(3)}_{\textbf{a},\textbf{b}}+{\cal V}^{(4)}_{\textbf{a},\textbf{b}}.

Lemma
7.1
follows from this decomposition and the simple fact

\sum_{b}\left|\Theta^{(B)}_{t}\right|_{ab}=O\left(1/\eta_{t}\right).

∎

If
\cal A
decays on the scale
\ell_{s}
, then
{\cal U}_{s,t,(+,-)}\circ\cal A
decays on the scale
\ell_{t}
.

Lemma 7.2

(Tail Estimates)
.

Recall

{\cal T}_{t}(\ell):=\frac{1}{(W\ell_{t}\eta_{t})^{2}}\exp\left(-\left|\ell/\ell_{t}\right|^{1/2}\right).

For
\boldsymbol{\sigma}=(+,-)
, assume that for
{\cal A}_{\textbf{a}}
,
\textbf{a}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}_{L}
and some large
D>0
, we have

{\cal A}_{\textbf{a}}\leq{\cal T}_{s}(a_{1}-a_{2})+W^{-D}.

Recalling
\cal U
defined in Definition
5.17
, we have

(7.3)

\displaystyle\left({\cal U}_{s,t,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\circ{\cal A}\right)_{\textbf{a}}

\displaystyle\prec{\cal T}_{t}(a_{1}-a_{2})+W^{-D}\cdot(\eta_{s}/\eta_{t})^{2},\quad{\rm if}\quad|a_{1}-a_{2}|\geq\ell_{t}^{*}:=(\log W)^{3/2}\ell_{t}.

We first give a heuristic argument for the proof. Notice that the key term in
{\cal U}_{s,t,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}
is

(t-s)^{2}\Theta_{t,a_{1}b_{1}}\Theta_{t,a_{2}b_{2}}\sim(\eta_{s}/\eta_{t})^{2}\omega_{t}(a_{1}-b_{1})\omega_{t}(a_{2}-b_{2});\quad\omega_{t}(a-b)=\eta_{t}\Theta_{t,ab}.

With this definition, we have the normalization
\sum_{b}\omega_{t}(b)=O(1)
.
The corresponding term in
\left({\cal U}_{s,t,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\circ{\cal A}\right)_{\textbf{a}}
is bounded by

(7.4)

\displaystyle(t-s)^{2}\sum_{b_{1},b_{2}}\Theta_{t,a_{1}b_{1}}\Theta_{t,a_{2}b_{2}}{\cal T}_{s}(b_{1}-b_{2})\sim(\eta_{s}/\eta_{t})^{2}\sum_{b_{1},b_{2}}\omega_{t}(a_{1}-b_{1})\omega_{t}(a_{2}-b_{2}){\cal T}_{s}(b_{1}-b_{2}).

Using
\omega_{t}\ast\omega_{t}\sim\omega_{t}
and denoting
a_{1}-a_{2}=x
, the last line is bounded by

\displaystyle(\eta_{s}/\eta_{t})^{2}(W\ell_{s}\eta_{s})^{-2}\ell_{t}^{-1}\sum_{b}\exp\left(-|x-b|/\ell_{t}-\sqrt{|b|/\ell_{s}}\right)

(7.5)

\displaystyle\leq(W\ell_{t}\eta_{t})^{-2}\big(\ell_{t}\ell_{s}^{-2})\sum_{b}\exp\left(-|x-b|/\ell_{t}-\sqrt{|b|/\ell_{s}}\right).

We can also assume that
b\leq\ell_{s}(\log W)^{2+1/4}
. Otherwise, the last line is exponentially small. By assumption,
x\geq(\log W)^{3/2}\ell_{t}
. If
(\log W)\ell_{s}\leq\ell_{t}
, then
|x-b|\geq(\log W)^{3/2}\ell_{t}/2
and the last line is exponentially small. Hence we can assume that
(\log W)\ell_{s}\geq\ell_{t}
. In this case, we use the trivial bound
-\sqrt{|b|/\ell_{s}}\leq-\sqrt{|b|/\ell_{t}}
and perform the
b
summation to bound (
7.1
) by

(7.6)

\displaystyle(\log W)^{C}(W\ell_{t}\eta_{t})^{-2}\exp\left(-\sqrt{x/\ell_{t}}\right).

Proof of Lemma
7.2
.

We split
\cal A
into two parts:

{\cal A}={\cal A}^{\cal T}+{\cal A}^{D},\quad{\cal A}^{\cal T}_{\textbf{a}}\leq{\cal T}_{s}(a_{1}-a_{2}),\quad{\cal A}^{D}\leq W^{-D}.

Since
\cal U
is linear, we only need to prove that

(7.7)

\displaystyle\left({\cal U}_{s,t,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\circ{\cal A}^{\cal T}\right)_{\textbf{a}}\prec\;{\cal T}_{t}(a_{1}-a_{2})+W^{-D},\quad

\displaystyle\left({\cal U}_{s,t,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\circ{\cal A}^{D}\right)_{\textbf{a}}\prec W^{-D}\cdot(\eta_{s}/\eta_{t})^{2}.

One can easily use the
\cal U_{\max\to\max}
bound in Lemma
7.1
to prove
the estimate on
{\cal A}^{D}
. It remains to prove the
{\cal A}^{\cal T}
part in (
7.7
).
Recall (
7.2
) and the bound

(7.8)

\displaystyle\|\Theta^{(B)}_{t}\|_{\max}\prec(\ell_{t}\eta_{t})^{-1}.

Due to the decay of
\Theta_{t}
and
{\cal A}^{\cal T}
, we can restrict
b
in
\sum_{\textbf{b}}
to

(7.9)

\left({\cal U}_{s,t}\circ{\cal A}^{\cal T}\right)_{\textbf{a}}=\sum_{b_{1},\,b_{2}}^{*}\;\prod_{i=1}^{2}(\Theta_{t}\Theta^{-1}_{s})_{a_{i}b_{i}}{\cal A}_{\textbf{b}}+W^{-D},

where

(7.10)

\displaystyle\sum_{b_{1},\,b_{2}}^{*}=\sum_{b_{1},\,b_{2}}{\bf 1}\left(|a_{i}-b_{i}|\leq\frac{1}{4}\ell^{*}_{t}\right)\cdot{\bf 1}\left(|b_{1}-b_{2}|\leq\log^{3}W\cdot\ell_{s}\right).

By assumption
|a_{1}-a_{2}|\geq\ell^{*}_{t}=(\log W)^{3/2}\cdot\ell_{t}
, the above summation is nontrivial only if

\ell_{s}\geq\ell_{t}\cdot(\log W)^{-2}.

In another words, if
|a_{1}-a_{2}|\geq\ell_{t}^{*}
, then for any
D>0
,

\ell_{s}\leq\ell_{t}\cdot(\log W)^{-2}\implies\left(\mathcal{U}_{s,t,\sigma}\circ\mathcal{A}\right)_{a}\leq W^{-D}.

From now on, we assume that

\ell_{t}\prec\ell_{s},\quad\sum_{b_{1},\,b_{2}}^{*}1\prec\ell_{t}\ell_{s},\quad\sum_{b_{1},\,b_{2}}^{*}\delta_{a_{1}b_{1}}\prec\ell_{s}.

Together with (
7.2
), (
7.8
),
{\cal A}^{\cal T}_{b_{1}b_{2}}\prec{\cal T}_{s}(b_{1}-b_{2})
, and
t-s=O(\eta_{s})
), we have

(7.11)

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{\textbf{b}}^{*}{\cal V}^{(1,2,3,4)}\circ{\cal A}^{\cal T}_{\textbf{b}}\right)\Bigg/{\cal T}_{t}(a_{1}-a_{2})\prec

\displaystyle\max_{\textbf{b}}^{*}\;\exp\left(\ell_{t}^{-1/2}\left(|a_{1}-a_{2}|^{1/2}-|b_{1}-b_{2}|^{1/2}\right)\right),

where
\max_{\textbf{b}}^{*}
satisfies the condition in (
7.10
). Under this condition, we have

|a_{1}-a_{2}|-|b_{1}-b_{2}|\leq\sum_{i}|a_{i}-b_{i}|\leq\frac{1}{2}(\log W)^{3/2}\ell_{t}.

Note that there is no absolute values on the left hand side.
It is easy to prove that

(7.12)

\max_{\textbf{b}}^{*}\left(|a_{1}-a_{2}|^{1/2}-|b_{1}-b_{2}|^{1/2}\right)\leq C\log^{3/4}W\cdot\ell^{1/2}_{t}.

Together with (
7.11
) and (
7.9
), we have proved Lemma
7.2
.
∎

Lemma 7.3

(
{\cal U}_{s,t}
on fast decay tensor)
.

Let
\cal A
be a tensor
\mathbb{Z}_{L}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}
,
n\geq 2
. We say
\cal A
is
(\tau,D)
decay at time
s
if for some fixed small
\tau>0
and large
D>0
,

(7.13)

\max_{i}\|a_{i}-a_{j}\|\geq\ell_{s}W^{\tau}\implies{\cal A}_{\textbf{a}}=O(W^{-D}),\quad\textbf{a}=(a_{1},a_{2}\cdots,a_{n}).

Then we have the following
\max\to\max
norm for
{\cal U}_{s,t,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}
for
n\geq 2
:

(7.14)

\displaystyle\left({\cal U}_{s,t,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\circ{\cal A}\right)_{\textbf{a}}\leq C_{n}W^{C_{n}\tau}\cdot\|{\cal A}\|_{\max}\cdot\left(\frac{\ell_{t}}{\ell_{s}}\right)\cdot\left(\frac{\ell_{s}\eta_{s}}{\ell_{t}\eta_{t}}\right)^{n}+W^{-D+C_{n}}.

Suppose either one of the following two assumptions hold:
Case 1: For some
k:

1\leq k\leq n
,

\sigma_{k}=\sigma_{k-1},\quad\boldsymbol{\sigma}=(\sigma_{1},\cdots,\sigma_{n}).

Case 2:
{\cal A}_{\textbf{b}}
has the sum zero property in the sense that

(7.15)

\displaystyle\sum_{a_{2},\,\cdots,\,a_{n}}{\cal A}_{\textbf{a}}=0,\quad\forall a_{1}\;.

Then we have the following stronger bound

(7.16)

\displaystyle\left({\cal U}_{s,t,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\circ{\cal A}\right)_{\textbf{a}}\leq W^{C_{n}\tau}\cdot\|{\cal A}\|_{\max}\cdot\left(\frac{\ell_{s}\eta_{s}}{\ell_{t}\eta_{t}}\right)^{n}+W^{-D+C_{n}}.

Lemma
7.3
can be understood as follows. The evolution kernel is approximately given by

(\eta_{s}/\eta_{t})^{n}{\otimes^{n}}\omega_{t}

where
\omega_{t}
is an
L_{1}
normalized convolution kernel of width
\ell_{t}
.
So operator norm of this kernel in
L_{\infty}
is
(\eta_{s}/\eta_{t})^{n}
. The decay length of

A
is
\ell_{s}<\ell_{t}
. Hence we gain a factor
\ell_{s}/\ell_{t}
for each summation restricted by
A
. Since there are
n-1
summations restricted by
A
, we gain
(\ell_{s}/\ell_{t})^{n-1}

and this explains (
7.14
). For the sum zero case, we gain an extra

(\ell_{s}/\ell_{t})
as we can sum by parts once and the ratio of smoothness between
\omega_{t}
and
A
is
(\ell_{s}/\ell_{t})
. For the case that
\sigma_{k}=\sigma_{k-1}
, the
\Theta
operator has become significantly smaller and we also gain an extra factor. The details will be given in the proof.

Proof of Lemma
7.3
.

We first prove (
7.14
). By definition of
{\cal U}_{s,t}
in (
5.17
) and (
5.18
), we have

(7.17)

\displaystyle\left(\mathcal{U}_{s,t,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\circ\mathcal{A}\right)_{a}=\sum_{b_{1},\ldots,b_{n}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\psi_{i}\cdot\mathcal{A}_{\boldsymbol{b}},\quad\boldsymbol{b}=\left(b_{1},\ldots,b_{n}\right),

(7.18)

\displaystyle\psi_{i}=\delta_{a_{i}b_{i}}+\Xi_{i},\quad\quad\Xi_{i}:=-(s-t)\xi_{i}\cdot\left(S^{(B)}\cdot\Theta^{(B)}_{t\xi_{i}}\right)_{a_{i}b_{i}},\quad\quad\xi_{i}=m(\sigma_{i})m(\sigma_{i+1}).

Recall the following identity for all
x
and
y
:

\prod_{i}(x_{i})-\prod_{i}(x_{i}-y_{i})=-\sum_{\emptyset\neq A\subset[[1,n]]}\left(\prod_{j\in A^{c}}x_{j}\right)\left(\prod_{j\in A}(-y_{i})\right).

Choosing
x_{i}=\psi_{i}
and
y_{i}=\delta_{a_{i}b_{i}}
, we have
x_{i}-y_{i}=\Xi_{i}
and

(7.19)

\displaystyle\prod_{i=1}^{n}\psi_{i}-\prod_{i=1}^{n}\ \Xi_{i}=\sum_{\emptyset\neq A\subset[[1,n]]}\left(\prod_{j\in A^{c}}\psi_{j}\right)(-1)^{|A|+1}\left(\prod_{j\in A}\delta_{a_{j}b_{j}}\right).

Assume that we have proved Lemma
7.3
for any
k<n
. Then by inductive assumption,

(7.20)

\displaystyle\sum_{\textbf{b}}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{n}\psi_{i}-\prod_{i=1}^{n}\Xi_{i}\right)\cdot{\cal A}_{\textbf{b}}\;\leq\;

\displaystyle C_{n}W^{C_{n}\tau}\|{\cal A}\|_{\max}\cdot\left(\frac{\ell_{t}}{\ell_{s}}\right)\cdot\left(\frac{\ell_{s}\eta_{s}}{\ell_{t}\eta_{t}}\right)^{n-1}+W^{-D+C_{n}}

\displaystyle\;\leq\;

\displaystyle C_{n}W^{C_{n}\tau}\|{\cal A}\|_{\max}\left(\frac{\ell_{s}\eta_{s}}{\ell_{t}\eta_{t}}\right)^{n}+W^{-D+C_{n}},

where we have used
\ell_{t}/\ell_{s}\leq\left(\ell_{s}\eta_{s}\right)/\left(\ell_{t}\eta_{t}\right)
.

For (
7.14
) we only need to bound

(7.21)

\displaystyle\sum_{\textbf{b}}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{n}\Xi_{i}\right)\cdot{\cal A}_{\textbf{b}}\leq C_{n}W^{C_{n}\tau}\cdot\|\mathcal{A}\|_{\max}\cdot\left(\frac{\ell_{t}}{\ell_{s}}\right)\cdot\left(\frac{\ell_{s}\eta_{s}}{\ell_{t}\eta_{t}}\right)^{n}+W^{-D+C_{n}}.

By definition of
\Theta^{(B)}
, we have

\Xi_{i}=O\left(\eta_{s}\,\ell_{t}^{-1}\eta_{t}^{-1}\right).

By
\ell_{s}
-decay property of
\cal A
in (
7.13
),

(7.22)

\displaystyle\sum_{\textbf{b}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\Xi_{i}\cdot{\cal A}_{\textbf{b}}\;\leq\;

\displaystyle CW^{\tau}\cdot\frac{\ell_{s}\eta_{s}}{\ell_{t}\eta_{t}}\max_{b_{n}}\cdot\left|\sum_{b_{1}\cdots b_{n-1}}\prod_{i=1}^{n-1}\Xi_{i}\cdot{\cal A}_{\textbf{b}}\right|+W^{-D+C}

\displaystyle\;\leq\;

\displaystyle C_{n}W^{C_{n}\tau}\left(\frac{\ell_{s}\eta_{s}}{\ell_{t}\eta_{t}}\right)^{n-1}\max_{b_{2},\cdots,b_{n}}\left|\sum_{b_{1}}\Xi_{1}{\cal A}_{\textbf{b}}\right|+W^{-D+C_{n}}.

By the decay of
\Theta^{(B)}_{t}
, we have

(7.23)

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{b_{1}}\Xi_{1}{\cal A}_{\textbf{b}}\right|\leq CW^{\tau}(\eta_{s}/\eta_{t})\|{\cal A}\|_{\max}+W^{-D}.

Together with (
7.22
) and (
7.20
), we have proved (
7.14
).

Next we prove Case 1 of (
7.16
). Without loss of generality, we assume that
\sigma_{1}=\sigma_{2}=+
. In this case,
\xi_{1}=m^{2}
, and thus
\sum_{b_{1}}|\Xi_{1}|=O(1)
. this implies that

\left|\sum_{b_{1}}\Xi_{1}{\cal A}_{\textbf{b}}\right|\leq C\|{\cal A}\|_{\max}.

Together with (
7.22
) and (
7.20
), we have proved (
7.16
) in this case.

We finally prove Case 2 of (
7.16
), i.e.,
\cal A
has the sum zero property (
7.15
).
Since we have proved (
7.16
) for the case 1, we can now assume that
\sigma_{i}\neq\sigma_{i+1}
for any
1\leq i\leq n
. It implies
\xi_{i}=|m|^{2}=1
(in (
7.18
)). For each
\Xi_{i}
with
i\geq 2
, we write it as

\Xi_{i}=\Xi^{0}_{i}+\Xi^{*}_{i}
where

\frac{\Xi^{0}_{i}}{-(s-t)\xi_{i}}:=\left(S^{(B)}\cdot\Theta^{(B)}_{t\xi_{i}}\right)_{a_{i}b_{1}},\quad\frac{\Xi^{*}_{i}}{-(s-t)\xi_{i}}:=\left(\left(S^{(B)}\cdot\Theta^{(B)}_{t\xi_{i}}\right)_{a_{i}b_{i}}-\left(S^{(B)}\cdot\Theta^{(B)}_{t\xi_{i}}\right)_{a_{i}b_{1}}\right).

Note that the subscript of
\Xi^{0}_{i}
is
a_{i}b_{1}
, but the one for
\Xi_{i}
is
a_{i}b_{1}
. Thus

\sum_{\textbf{b}}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{n}\Xi_{i}\right)\cdot{\cal A}_{\textbf{b}}=\sum_{\textbf{b}}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{n}\left(\Xi^{0}_{i}+\Xi^{*}_{i}\right)\right)\cdot{\cal A}_{\textbf{b}}.

After expanding the
\prod_{i}
, the leading term disappears due to the sum zero property (and the fact that
\Xi_{i}^{0}
is independent of
b_{2},\cdots,b_{n}
), namely,

\sum_{\textbf{b}}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{n}\Xi^{0}_{i}\right)\cdot{\cal A}_{\textbf{b}}=0.

For the other terms, we bound them by

\left|\Xi_{i}^{0}\right|\leq C\frac{\eta_{s}}{\ell_{t}\eta_{t}},\quad\quad\left|\Xi_{i}^{*}\right|\leq C\frac{\eta_{s}}{\ell_{t}\,\eta_{t}}\cdot\frac{|b_{i}-b_{1}|}{\ell_{t}}.

By the decay property of
\cal A
(
7.13
), the main contribution tothe last equation comes from
|b_{i}-b_{1}|\leq W^{\tau}\ell_{s}
. Therefore, we obtain an improved bound for (
7.22
), i.e.,

(7.24)

\displaystyle\sum_{\textbf{b}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\Xi_{i}\cdot{\cal A}_{\textbf{b}}\;\leq\;

\displaystyle C_{n}W^{C_{n}\tau}\left(\frac{\ell_{s}\eta_{s}}{\ell_{t}\eta_{t}}\right)^{n-1}\cdot\frac{\ell_{s}}{\ell_{t}}\cdot\max_{b_{2},\cdots,b_{n}}\left|\sum_{b_{1}}\Xi_{1}{\cal A}_{\textbf{b}}\right|+W^{-D+C_{n}}.

Together with (
7.23
), we have proved Case 2 of (
7.16
). This completes the proof of Lemma
7.3
.

∎

7.2
Auxiliary proofs for universality

In this subsection we prove (
2.27
) and (
2.26
) for
H_{t}
defined in (
2.19
). This notation is particular for universality proof and different from
H_{t}
defined in (
2.34
) for the main proof) For the matrix
H_{t}
, the case
t=0
corresponds to our primary result for the band matrix resolvent. The cases for
0<t<t_{U}
can be handled similarly to the case
t=t_{U}
. Thus, for simplicity, we only provide the proof for the case
t=t_{U}
, i.e., the estimate for
H_{t_{U}}
. We define the random matrix

(7.25)

\widetilde{H}:=H_{t_{U}}

By definition,
\widetilde{H}
has entry variances given by

\mathbb{E}\big|\widetilde{H}_{xy}\big|^{2}=\mathbb{E}\big|\left(H_{t_{U}}\right)_{xy}\big|^{2}=\widetilde{S}_{xy}=(1-\zeta_{U})S_{xy}+\frac{\zeta_{U}}{N},\quad\zeta_{U}\sim N^{-1+\tau_{U}},\quad\zeta_{U}=1-e^{-t_{U}}

In our main proof, we use the flow

z^{(E)}_{t}=E+(1-t)m^{(E)},\quad 0\leq t\leq 1.

Then for
z\in\mathbb{C}
, we choose
t_{0}
and
E
such that

z=t_{0}^{-1/2}z_{t_{0}}^{(E)},

and study
G(z)
via
t_{0}^{1/2}\cdot G^{(E)}_{t_{0}}
as in (
2.39
). Here for fixed
z
, we keep the same stochastic flow for
z^{(E)}_{t}
, but the flow for
\widetilde{H}_{t}
is defined by
\widetilde{H}_{0}=0
and

d\widetilde{H}_{t,ij}=\begin{cases}\sqrt{S_{ij}}\cdot d{\cal B}_{t,ij}&0\leq t\leq t_{1}:=(1-\zeta_{U})t_{0},\\[6.0pt]
N^{-1/2}\cdot d{\cal B}_{t,ij}&t_{1}\leq t\leq t_{0}.\end{cases}

It is easy to check that for any fixed
z
satisfying
z=t_{0}^{-1/2}z_{t_{0}}^{(E)}
,

(7.26)

\displaystyle(\widetilde{H}-z)^{-1}\;\overset{d}{\sim}\;t_{0}^{1/2}(\widetilde{H}_{t_{0}}-z_{t_{0}})^{-1}

Define
\widetilde{G}_{t}
and
\widetilde{{\cal L}}_{t}
with
\widetilde{H}_{t}
as in Definitions
2.7
and
2.9
. By definition for
t<t_{1}
,

\widetilde{H}_{t}\;\overset{d}{\sim}\;H_{t},\quad\widetilde{{\cal L}}_{t}\;\overset{d}{\sim}\;{{\cal L}}_{t},\quad t\leq t_{1}.

Here
H_{t}
and
{{\cal L}}_{t}
are defined in (
2.34
) and (
2.41
) respectively. We aim to estimate
\widetilde{{\cal L}}_{t}
with
\widetilde{{\cal K}}_{t}
for
t\in[t_{1},t_{0}]
. Recall
\widetilde{{\cal K}}
is the solution to the primitive equation in Definition
2.12
. For
t\in[t_{1},t_{0}]
the flow equation becomes

\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{\widetilde{K}}_{t,\sigma,a}=W\cdot\sum_{1\leq k<l\leq n}\sum_{a,b}\left(\mathcal{G}_{k,l}^{(a),L}\circ\mathcal{\widetilde{K}}_{t,\sigma,a}\right)\left(S_{GUE}^{(B)}\right)_{ab}\left(\mathcal{G}_{k,l}^{(b),R}\circ\mathcal{\widetilde{K}}_{t,\sigma,a}\right),\quad t\in[t_{1},t_{0}]

with

\left(S^{(B)}_{GUE}\right)_{ab}=1/L,\quad\mathcal{\widetilde{K}}_{t,\sigma,a}=\mathcal{{K}}_{t,\sigma,a},\quad t\in[0,t_{1}].

To prove (
2.27
) and (
2.26
), we only need to focus on the following case: for small enough
\tau_{U}
with

z=E+\eta i,\quad\quad N^{-1+2\tau_{U}}\leq\eta\leq N^{-1+\mathfrak{c}/3},

and
t\in[t_{1},t_{0}]
, we have

(7.27)

\displaystyle\max_{\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\left|{\cal\widetilde{L}}_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\right|

\displaystyle\;\prec\;(N\eta_{t})^{-1},

(7.28)

\displaystyle\max_{\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\left|{\cal\widetilde{L}}_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}-{\cal\widetilde{K}}_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\right|

\displaystyle\;\prec\;(N\eta_{t})^{-2},

(7.29)

\displaystyle\max_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}\in\{+,-\}^{2}}\max_{\textbf{a}}\left|\mathbb{E}{\cal\widetilde{L}}_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}-\mathbb{E}{\cal\widetilde{K}}_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\right|

\displaystyle\;\prec\;(N\eta_{t})^{-3}.

In this setting (
\im z=\eta
)

(7.30)

1-t_{0}\sim 1-t_{1}\sim\eta,\quad\quad t_{0}-t_{1}\sim N^{-1+\tau_{U}},\quad\quad N^{-1+2\tau_{U}}\leq\eta\leq N^{-1+\mathfrak{c}/3}.

Notice that
t_{0}-t_{1}\ll\eta
. By Lemma
2.17
and
2.18
, for
t\in[0,t_{1}]
,

(7.31)

\displaystyle\max_{\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\left|{\cal\widetilde{L}}_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}-{\cal\widetilde{K}}_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\right|\prec(W\ell_{t}\eta_{t})^{-n},\quad\eta_{t}:=\im z_{t},

\displaystyle\max_{\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\left|{\cal\widetilde{K}}_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\right|\prec(W\ell_{t}\eta_{t})^{-n+1},\quad\quad\max_{\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\left|{\cal\widetilde{L}}_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\right|\prec(W\ell_{t}\eta_{t})^{-n+1}.

With assumption (
7.30
), we have
\ell_{t_{1}}=L
.
Then for
t=t_{1}
,

(7.32)

\displaystyle\max_{\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\left|{\cal\widetilde{L}}_{t_{1},\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}-{\cal\widetilde{K}}_{t_{1},\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\right|\prec(N\eta_{t_{1}})^{-n},\quad\max_{\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\left(\left|{\cal\widetilde{K}}_{t_{1},\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\right|+\left|{\cal\widetilde{L}}_{t_{1},\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\right|\right)\prec(N\eta_{t_{1}})^{-n+1}.

For
t_{1}\leq t\leq t_{0}
, we will have a new loop hierarchy and primitive equation. The new version of these equations are the same as the standard versions in (
2.45
) and (
2.48
) except that
S^{(B)}
and
S
are replaced by
S^{(B)}_{GUE}
and
S_{GUE}
respectively, i.e.,

S^{(B)}\to S^{(B)}_{GUE},\quad\quad\left(S^{(B)}_{GUE}\right)_{ab}=1/L,\quad\quad S\to S_{GUE},\quad\quad\left(S_{GUE}\right)_{xy}=1/N.

Thus we can bound
\mathcal{\widetilde{K}}
by

(7.33)

\displaystyle\left|\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{\widetilde{K}}_{t,\sigma,a}\right|

\displaystyle\;\leq\;C_{n}N\max_{ab}\max_{kl}\left|\mathcal{G}_{k,l}^{(a),L}\circ\mathcal{\widetilde{K}}_{t,\sigma,a}\right|\cdot\left|\mathcal{G}_{k,l}^{(b),R}\circ\mathcal{\widetilde{K}}_{t,\sigma,a}\right|,\quad t\in[t_{1},t_{0}],

(7.34)

\displaystyle\;\leq\;C_{n}\cdot N\sum_{2\leq k\leq n}\left(\max_{\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\left|\mathcal{\widetilde{K}}^{(k)}_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\right|\right)\cdot\left(\max_{\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\left|\mathcal{\widetilde{K}}^{(n-k+2)}_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\right|\right).

Then for
t\in[t_{1},t_{0}]
,

(7.35)

\displaystyle|\mathcal{\widetilde{K}}_{t,\sigma,a}|\leq|\mathcal{\widetilde{K}}_{t_{1},\sigma,a}|+C_{n}N\cdot(t-t_{1})\cdot\sum_{2\leq k\leq n}\left(\max_{\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\left|\mathcal{\widetilde{K}}^{(k)}_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\right|\right)\cdot\left(\max_{\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\left|\mathcal{\widetilde{K}}^{(n-k+2)}_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\right|\right)

By (
7.30
),
t-t_{1}\leq t_{0}-t_{1}
and

N(t-t_{1})\leq N\eta_{t}\cdot N^{-\tau_{U}},\quad t\in[t_{1},t_{0}].

By a continuity argument and the initial estimate (
7.32
), we have

(7.36)

{\cal\widetilde{K}}_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\prec(N\eta_{t})^{-n+1},\quad t\in[t_{1},t_{0}].

To bouond
\cal L
, we apply the main flow estimates from Lemmas
5.3
and
5.5
with
s=t_{1}
and
t\in[t_{1},t_{0}]
. Together with Lemma
7.1
which implies that

\|{\cal U}_{t_{1},t,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\|_{\max\to\max}\prec(\eta_{t_{1}}/\eta_{t})^{n}\prec 1,

we have

\displaystyle(\mathcal{\widetilde{L}}-\mathcal{\widetilde{K}})_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\;\prec\;\;

\displaystyle\max_{\boldsymbol{\sigma},\,\textbf{a}}\left|(\mathcal{\widetilde{L}}-\mathcal{\widetilde{K}})_{t_{1},\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\right|+\int_{t_{1}}^{t}\left(\mathcal{U}_{u,t,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\circ\mathcal{\widetilde{E}}^{(M)}_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\right)_{\textbf{a}}du

(7.37)

\displaystyle+\int_{t_{1}}^{t}\max_{\boldsymbol{\sigma},\,\textbf{a}}\left(\sum_{l_{\mathcal{K}}>2}\left|\Big[\mathcal{\widetilde{K}}\sim(\mathcal{\widetilde{L}}-\mathcal{\widetilde{K}})\Big]^{l_{\mathcal{K}}}_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\right|+\left|\mathcal{\widetilde{E}}^{((\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K})\times(\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K}))}_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\right|+\left|\mathcal{\widetilde{E}}^{(\widetilde{G})}_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\right|\right)du

and

(7.38)

\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{t_{1}}^{\tau}\left(\mathcal{U}_{u,t,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\circ\mathcal{\widetilde{E}}^{(M)}_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\right)_{\textbf{a}}du\right]^{2p}\leq C_{n,p}\;\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{t_{1}}^{\tau}\max_{\boldsymbol{\sigma},\,\textbf{a}}\left(\mathcal{\widetilde{E}}\otimes\mathcal{\widetilde{E}}\right)_{u,\,\boldsymbol{\sigma},{\textbf{a}},{\textbf{a}}}du\right)^{p}.

We have the following estimates

•

Similar to (
5.13
) and (
5.2
), with
S^{(B)}
replaced by
S^{(B)}_{GUE}
, we have

(7.39)

\sum_{l_{\mathcal{K}}>2}\left|\Big[\mathcal{\widetilde{K}}\sim(\mathcal{\widetilde{L}}-\mathcal{\widetilde{K}})\Big]^{l_{\mathcal{K}}}_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\right|\prec N\cdot\sum_{2<k\leq n}(N\eta_{u})^{k-1}\cdot\max_{\boldsymbol{\sigma},\,\textbf{a}}\left|(\mathcal{\widetilde{L}}-\mathcal{\widetilde{K}})^{(n-k+2)}_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\right|

and

(7.40)

\mathcal{\widetilde{E}}^{((\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K})\times(\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{K}))}_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\prec N\cdot\sum_{2\leq k\leq n}\max_{\boldsymbol{\sigma},\,\textbf{a}}\left|(\mathcal{\widetilde{L}}-\mathcal{\widetilde{K}})^{(k)}_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\right|\cdot\max_{\boldsymbol{\sigma},\,\textbf{a}}\left|(\mathcal{\widetilde{L}}-\mathcal{\widetilde{K}})^{(n-k+2)}_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\right|

where superscript
(k)
represents the rank, i.e.,
{\cal L}^{(n)}
is an
n
-loop.

•

For
\mathcal{\widetilde{E}}^{(\widetilde{G})}
, similarly one can prove (
4.5
) for
\widetilde{G}_{t}
. Then

(7.41)

\displaystyle\mathcal{\widetilde{E}}^{(\widetilde{G})}_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\;\prec\;

\displaystyle N\cdot\max_{\boldsymbol{\sigma},\,\textbf{a}}\left|\mathcal{\widetilde{L}}^{(2)}_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\right|\cdot\max_{\boldsymbol{\sigma},\,\textbf{a}}\left|\mathcal{\widetilde{L}}^{(n+1)}_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\right|

By Cauchy Schwartz’s inequality we can bound
n+1
loop by a product of

2
loop and a
2n
loop. On the other hand, with (
5.117
), a
2n
loop can be bounded by the square of an
n
loop. Inserting all these bounds into the previous inequality, we have

(7.42)

\displaystyle\mathcal{\widetilde{E}}^{(\widetilde{G})}_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\;\prec\;

\displaystyle N\cdot\max_{\boldsymbol{\sigma},\,\textbf{a}}\left|\mathcal{\widetilde{L}}^{(2)}_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\right|^{3/2}\cdot\max_{\boldsymbol{\sigma},\,\textbf{a}}\left|\mathcal{\widetilde{L}}^{(n)}_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\right|

•

For
\mathcal{\widetilde{E}}\otimes\mathcal{\widetilde{E}}
term, recall the loops in Definition
5.4
. The
S^{(B)}
in
(\mathcal{\widetilde{E}}\otimes\mathcal{\widetilde{E}})^{(k)}
is replaced by
S^{(B)}_{GUE}
in
(\mathcal{\widetilde{E}}\otimes\mathcal{\widetilde{E}})^{(k)}
. Since
S_{GUE}^{(B)}
is a constant matrix, we can use Ward’s identity to sum up the
b
and
b^{\prime}
in the definition of
(\mathcal{\widetilde{E}}\otimes\mathcal{\widetilde{E}})^{(k)}
and obtain that

(7.43)

\left(\mathcal{\widetilde{E}}\otimes\mathcal{\widetilde{E}}\right)_{u,\,\boldsymbol{\sigma},{\textbf{a}},{\textbf{a}}}\prec N^{-1}\cdot\eta_{u}^{-2}\cdot\max_{\boldsymbol{\sigma},\,\textbf{a}}\left|\mathcal{\widetilde{L}}^{(2n)}_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\right|

Then use (
5.117
), we can bound
2n
loop with
n
loops

(7.44)

\left(\mathcal{\widetilde{E}}\otimes\mathcal{\widetilde{E}}\right)_{u,\,\boldsymbol{\sigma},{\textbf{a}},{\textbf{a}}}\prec N^{-1}\cdot\eta_{u}^{-2}\cdot\max_{\boldsymbol{\sigma},\,\textbf{a}}\left|\mathcal{\widetilde{L}}^{(n)}_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\right|^{2}

Combining (
7.32
), (
7.2
), (
7.38
), (
7.39
), (
7.40
), (
7.42
) and (
7.44
) we obtain that

\displaystyle(\mathcal{\widetilde{L}}-\mathcal{\widetilde{K}})_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\;\prec\;\;

\displaystyle\left(N|t-t_{1}|\right)\cdot\sup_{u\in[t_{1},t]}\;\sum_{2\leq k\leq n}\left((N\eta_{u})^{k-1}+\max_{\boldsymbol{\sigma},\,\textbf{a}}\left|(\mathcal{\widetilde{L}}-\mathcal{\widetilde{K}})^{(k)}_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\right|\right)\cdot\max_{\boldsymbol{\sigma},\,\textbf{a}}\left|(\mathcal{\widetilde{L}}-\mathcal{\widetilde{K}})^{(n-k+2)}_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\right|

(7.45)

\displaystyle\;+\;(N\eta_{t})^{-n}+\left(N|t-t_{1}|\right)\cdot\sup_{u\in[t_{1},t]}\max_{\boldsymbol{\sigma},\,\textbf{a}}\left|\mathcal{\widetilde{L}}^{(2)}_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\right|^{3/2}\cdot\max_{\boldsymbol{\sigma},\,\textbf{a}}\left|\mathcal{\widetilde{L}}^{(n)}_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\right|

\displaystyle\;+\;|t-t_{1}|^{1/2}\cdot\sup_{u\in[t_{1},t]}\left(N^{-1}\eta_{u}^{-2}\right)^{1/2}\cdot\max_{\boldsymbol{\sigma},\,\textbf{a}}\left|\mathcal{\widetilde{L}}^{(n)}_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\right|

By writing
\mathcal{\widetilde{L}}=(\mathcal{\widetilde{L}}-\mathcal{\widetilde{K}})+\mathcal{\widetilde{K}}
and using the bound (
7.36
) on
\mathcal{\widetilde{K}}
,
the last inequality becomes an inequality on
\mathcal{\widetilde{L}}
.
By assumption (
7.30
), we have

\eta_{u}\sim 1-u\geq N^{1-2\tau_{U}}\gg N^{1-\tau_{U}}\sim|t_{0}-t_{1}|\geq|t-t_{1}|.

With a continuity argument and an induction on
n\geq 2
, this inequality thus implies the estimate (
7.27
) on
\mathcal{\widetilde{L}}
.

Combining (
7.32
), (
7.2
), (
7.38
), (
7.39
), (
7.40
), (
7.41
) and (
7.43
), we obtain that

\displaystyle(\mathcal{\widetilde{L}}-\mathcal{\widetilde{K}})_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\;\prec\;\;

\displaystyle\left(N|t-t_{1}|\right)\cdot\sup_{u\in[t_{1},t]}\;\sum_{2\leq k\leq n}\left((N\eta_{u})^{k-1}+\max_{\boldsymbol{\sigma},\,\textbf{a}}\left|(\mathcal{\widetilde{L}}-\mathcal{\widetilde{K}})^{(k)}_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\right|\right)\cdot\max_{\boldsymbol{\sigma},\,\textbf{a}}\left|(\mathcal{\widetilde{L}}-\mathcal{\widetilde{K}})^{(n-k+2)}_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\right|

(7.46)

\displaystyle\;+\;(N\eta_{t})^{-n}+\left(N|t-t_{1}|\right)\cdot\sup_{u\in[t_{1},t]}\max_{\boldsymbol{\sigma},\,\textbf{a}}\left|\mathcal{\widetilde{L}}^{(2)}_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\right|\cdot\max_{\boldsymbol{\sigma},\,\textbf{a}}\left|\mathcal{\widetilde{L}}^{(n+1)}_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\right|

\displaystyle\;+\;|t-t_{1}|^{1/2}\cdot\sup_{u\in[t_{1},t]}\left(N^{-1}\eta_{u}^{-2}\right)^{1/2}\cdot\max_{\boldsymbol{\sigma},\,\textbf{a}}\left|\mathcal{\widetilde{L}}^{(2n)}_{u,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\textbf{a}}\right|^{1/2}.

Inserting the
\cal\widetilde{L}
bound (
7.27
) into the above
\cal\widetilde{L}-\cal\widetilde{K}
estimate, we have proved that (
7.28
) holds.
Finally (
7.29
) can be proved by following the proof of (
2.80
) in subsection
5.8
. Since

\ell_{u}\sim L,\quad\eta_{u}\sim\eta_{t_{0}},\quad t\in[t_{1},t_{0}]

in the current setting
we can prove (
7.29
) similarly to (
2.80
) in subsection
5.8
with very minor changes.

We now prove (
2.26
) and (
2.27
) for
\widetilde{H}
(defined in (
7.25
)) by using (
7.27
), (
7.28
), (
7.29
) and the identity (
7.26
). First, (
2.26
) clearly follows from (
7.26
) and (
7.28
). To prove (
2.27
), by (
7.29
) we have the following estimates similar to (
2.8
) and (
2.9
), namely, with the notations

\widetilde{G}=(\widetilde{H}-z)^{-1},\quad\im z=\eta=N^{-1+\mathfrak{c}/3},

and

\widetilde{S}^{(B)}_{xy}=(1-\zeta_{U})S^{(B)}_{xy}+\frac{\zeta_{U}}{L},\quad\zeta_{U}\sim N^{-1+\tau_{U}},\quad\zeta_{U}=1-e^{-t_{U}},

we have

(7.47)

\displaystyle\max_{a,b}\left|\mathbb{E}\tr\widetilde{G}E_{a}\widetilde{G}^{\dagger}E_{b}-W^{-1}\left(\frac{|m|^{2}}{1-|m|^{2}\widetilde{S}^{(B)}}\right)_{ab}\right|\leq\left(N\eta\right)^{-3}\cdot W^{\tau},

\displaystyle\max_{a,b}\left|\mathbb{E}\tr\widetilde{G}E_{a}\widetilde{G}E_{b}-W^{-1}\left(\frac{m^{2}}{1-m^{2}\widetilde{S}^{(B)}}\right)_{ab}\right|\leq\left(N\eta\right)^{-3}\cdot W^{\tau}.

We have thus proved the conclusion of Theorem
2.4
except that
S^{(B)}
is replaced by
\widetilde{S}^{(B)}
. We claimed that the proof for (
2.12
)
from (
2.2
) to (
2.17
) can be used
to derive the (
2.27
) for
\widetilde{H}
with only very minor modifications.
By definition of
\widetilde{S}^{(B)}
, we can remove the zero mode in
\widetilde{S}^{(B)}
and write

\left|\left(\frac{1}{1-\xi\cdot\widetilde{S}^{(B)}}\right)_{ab}-\left(\frac{1}{1-\xi\cdot\widetilde{S}^{(B)}}\right)_{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}}\right|=\left|\left(\frac{1}{1-\xi\cdot(1-\zeta_{U}){S}^{(B)}}\right)_{ab}-\left(\frac{1}{1-\xi\cdot(1-\zeta_{U}){S}^{(B)}}\right)_{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}}\right|.

In both
\xi=m^{2}
or
|m^{2}|
we have

|1-\xi|\sim|1-\xi(1-\zeta_{U})|.

We can now follow the argument from (
2.2
) to (
2.17
) to prove
(
2.27
) for
\widetilde{H}
(
7.29
). This completes the proof of (
2.27
).

Appendix A
G
-chains

Besides the loop of
G
entries defined in Definition
2.9
, we can also bound certain
chains
of
G
entries. Although the following results were not used in the proof of our main theorem, they might be useful in the future applications of the methods discussed in this paper.

Definition A.1

(
G
-chains)
.

Let

\boldsymbol{\sigma}=(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\ldots,\sigma_{n}),\quad\mathbf{a}=(a_{1},a_{2},\ldots,a_{n-1}),\quad\sigma_{k}\in\{+,-\},\quad a_{k}\in\mathbb{Z}_{L}.

Following the structure in (
5.114
), we define a
G
-chain
as

(A.1)

\mathcal{C}_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\mathbf{a}}=G_{t}(\sigma_{1})E_{a_{1}}G_{t}(\sigma_{2})E_{a_{2}}\cdots E_{a_{n-1}}G_{t}(\sigma_{n}).

By definition,
\mathcal{C}_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\mathbf{a}}
is an
N\times N
matrix. We classify its entries as

\left(\mathcal{C}_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\mathbf{a}}\right)_{ij}\sim\begin{cases}\text{off-diagonal terms of }\mathcal{C}_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\mathbf{a}},&\text{if }i\neq j,\\
\text{diagonal terms of }\mathcal{C}_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\mathbf{a}},&\text{if }i=j.\end{cases}

Before presenting the lemmas for estimating
G
-chains, we provide a heuristic argument for the following bounds on
G
-chains:

(A.2)

\left(\mathcal{C}_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\mathbf{a}}\right)_{ii}\prec(W\ell_{t}\eta_{t})^{-n+1},\quad\left(\mathcal{C}_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\mathbf{a}}\right)_{ij}\prec(W\ell_{t}\eta_{t})^{-n+1/2}.

A
G
-chain can be converted into a
G
-loop by multiplication with an
E_{a}
operator followed by taking the trace:

\mathcal{L}_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\mathbf{a}^{\prime}}=\langle\mathcal{C}_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\mathbf{a}}E_{a}\rangle,\quad\mathbf{a}^{\prime}=(a_{1},a_{2},\ldots,a_{n-1},a).

Assuming that
\left(\mathcal{C}_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\mathbf{a}}\right)_{ii}
is of comparable size for all
i\in\mathcal{I}_{a}
, we obtain from (
2.77
)

\left(\mathcal{C}_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\mathbf{a}}\right)_{ii}\sim\mathcal{L}_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\mathbf{a}^{\prime}}\sim(W\ell_{t}\eta_{t})^{-n+1}.

Similarly, a different
G
-loop can be constructed by multiplying a
G
-chain with two operators
E_{a}
and
E_{b}
and taking the trace:

\mathcal{L}_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime}}=\langle E_{a}\cdot\mathcal{C}_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\mathbf{a}}\cdot E_{b}\cdot\mathcal{C}^{\dagger}_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\mathbf{a}}\rangle,

where

\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{\prime\prime}=(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\ldots,\sigma_{n},\overline{\sigma}_{n},\overline{\sigma}_{n-1},\ldots,\overline{\sigma}_{1}),\quad\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime}=(a_{1},a_{2},\ldots,a_{n-1},b,a_{n-1},\ldots,a_{1},a).

Assuming that
\left(\mathcal{C}_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\mathbf{a}}\right)_{ij}
has a typical size across all
i\in\mathcal{I}_{a}
,
j\in\mathcal{I}_{b}
, we deduce from (
2.77
) that

\left(\mathcal{C}_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\mathbf{a}}\right)_{ij}\sim\left(\mathcal{L}_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{a}^{\prime\prime}}\right)^{1/2}\sim(W\ell_{t}\eta_{t})^{-n+1/2}.

Lemma A.2

(
n
-chain estimate)
.

Under the assumptions of Lemma
2.18
, for any fixed
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
\boldsymbol{\sigma}\in\{+,-\}^{n}
, we have

(A.3)

\displaystyle\max_{\mathbf{a}}\max_{i}\left|\left(\mathcal{C}_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\mathbf{a}}\right)_{ii}\right|

\displaystyle\prec\left(W\ell_{t}\eta_{t}\right)^{-n+1},

(A.4)

\displaystyle\max_{\mathbf{a}}\max_{i\neq j}\left|\left(\mathcal{C}_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\mathbf{a}}\right)_{ij}\right|

\displaystyle\prec\left(W\ell_{t}\eta_{t}\right)^{-n+1/2}.

The proof relies on a standard decomposition widely used for Wigner matrices (see, e.g.,
[
23
]
) and random band matrices (e.g.,
[
19
]
).

Proof of Lemma
A.2
.

We begin by introducing the following shorthand:

(A.5)

\displaystyle\Xi_{n}^{(\mathrm{d})}:=\Xi_{t,n}^{(\mathcal{C},\mathrm{diag})}

\displaystyle:=\max_{\boldsymbol{\sigma},\mathbf{a}}\max_{i}\left|\left(\mathcal{C}_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\mathbf{a}}\right)_{ii}\right|\cdot(W\ell_{t}\eta_{t})^{n-1}\cdot\mathbf{1}\left(\boldsymbol{\sigma}\in\{+,-\}^{n}\right),

(A.6)

\displaystyle\Xi_{n}^{(\mathrm{o})}:=\Xi_{t,n}^{(\mathcal{C},\mathrm{off})}

\displaystyle:=\max_{\boldsymbol{\sigma},\mathbf{a}}\max_{i\neq j}\left|\left(\mathcal{C}_{t,\boldsymbol{\sigma},\mathbf{a}}\right)_{ij}\right|\cdot(W\ell_{t}\eta_{t})^{n-1/2}\cdot\mathbf{1}\left(\boldsymbol{\sigma}\in\{+,-\}^{n}\right).

For the base case
n=1
, Theorem
2.3
implies

\Xi_{1}^{(\mathrm{d})}+\Xi_{1}^{(\mathrm{o})}\prec 1.

Applying the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality to the diagonal part of a 2-chain yields

\Xi_{2}^{(\mathrm{d})}\prec\left(\Xi_{1}^{(\mathrm{d})}\right)^{2}+\left(\Xi_{1}^{(\mathrm{o})}\right)^{2}\prec 1.

We now prove the inductive step. Suppose that for all
n\geq 2
, we have the bound:

(A.7)

\displaystyle\left(\max_{k\leq n-1}\Xi_{k}^{(\mathrm{o})}+\max_{k\leq 2n-2}\Xi_{k}^{(\mathrm{d})}\right)\prec 1\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\left(\max_{k\leq n}\Xi_{k}^{(\mathrm{o})}+\max_{k\leq 2n}\Xi_{k}^{(\mathrm{d})}\right)\prec 1.

This inductive bound immediately implies the estimates (
A.3
) and (
A.4
).
To proceed, define the
n
-chain

(A.8)

\mathcal{C}_{n}:=G_{1}E_{a_{1}}G_{2}\cdots G_{n-1}E_{a_{n-1}}G_{n}.

Let
\mathcal{C}_{n}^{(i)}
denote the
n
-chain where all
G_{k}
with
k\geq 2
are replaced by
G_{k}^{(i)}
(defined in (
4.6
)):

(A.9)

\mathcal{C}_{n}^{(i)}:=G_{1}E_{a_{1}}G^{(i)}_{2}\cdots G^{(i)}_{n-1}E_{a_{n-1}}G^{(i)}_{n}.

Similarly, let
\mathcal{C}_{n}^{(ii)}
be the
n
-chain with all
G_{k}
replaced by
G_{k}^{(i)}
, including the first:

(A.10)

\mathcal{C}_{n}^{(ii)}:=G^{(i)}_{1}E_{a_{1}}G^{(i)}_{2}\cdots G^{(i)}_{n-1}E_{a_{n-1}}G^{(i)}_{n}.

Under the assumption of (
A.7
), we claim the following perturbation estimates hold for
n\geq 2
:

(A.11)

i\neq j,\qquad\left(\mathcal{C}_{n}\right)_{ij}-\left(\mathcal{C}^{(i)}_{n}\right)_{ij}\prec(W\ell\eta)^{-n+1/2},

and

(A.12)

\left\langle\mathcal{C}_{n}^{(ii)}E_{a}\mathcal{C}_{n}^{(ii)\;\dagger}E_{b}\right\rangle-\left\langle\mathcal{C}_{n}E_{a}\mathcal{C}_{n}^{\dagger}E_{b}\right\rangle\prec\left(\Xi_{2n}^{(\mathrm{d})}+1\right)\cdot(W\ell\eta)^{-2n+1/2}.

Moreover, if in addition
\Xi_{2n-1}^{(\mathrm{d})}+\Xi_{2n}^{(\mathrm{d})}\prec 1
, then we also have

(A.13)

i\neq j,\qquad\left(\mathcal{C}_{n}^{(ii)}E_{a}\mathcal{C}_{n}^{(ii)\;\dagger}\right)_{jj}-\left(\mathcal{C}_{n}E_{a}\mathcal{C}_{n}^{\dagger}\right)_{jj}\prec\left(\left(\Xi_{n}^{(\mathrm{o})}\right)^{2}+1\right)\cdot(W\ell\eta)^{-2n+1/2}.

We postpone the proofs of these perturbative estimates to the end of this section.

We now prove

(A.14)

\Xi^{(\mathrm{d})}_{2n-1}+\Xi^{(\mathrm{d})}_{2n}\prec 1,

using (
A.11
) and (
A.12
). By the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality and the assumption in (
A.7
), it suffices to control the case
2n
. Without loss of generality, we may assume that the
2n
-chain
\mathcal{C}
is symmetric and of the form

\left(\mathcal{C}_{n}E_{a}\mathcal{C}_{n}^{\dagger}\right)_{ii}.

We decompose this expression as

\left(\mathcal{C}_{n}E_{a}\mathcal{C}_{n}^{\dagger}\right)_{ii}=W^{-1}\mathcal{C}_{n;ii}\cdot\mathcal{C}^{\dagger}_{n;ii}+\sum_{j\neq i}\mathcal{C}_{n;ij}\cdot E_{a}(j)\cdot\mathcal{C}^{\dagger}_{n;ji},

and similarly for
\mathcal{C}_{n}^{(i)}E_{a}\left(\mathcal{C}_{n}^{(i)}\right)^{\dagger}
. Then,

\displaystyle\left(\mathcal{C}_{n}E_{a}\mathcal{C}_{n}^{\dagger}\right)_{ii}-\left(\mathcal{C}_{n}^{(i)}E_{a}\left(\mathcal{C}_{n}^{(i)}\right)^{\dagger}\right)_{ii}\;\prec\;

\displaystyle(W\ell\eta)^{-2n+1}\left(\Xi_{n}^{(\mathrm{d})}\right)^{2}+\max_{j\neq i}\left|\left(\mathcal{C}_{n}\right)_{ij}-\left(\mathcal{C}_{n}^{(i)}\right)_{ij}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle+\left(\sum_{j\neq i}\left|\mathcal{C}_{n;ij}\right|^{2}E_{a}(j)\right)^{1/2}\cdot\max_{j\neq i}\left|\left(\mathcal{C}_{n}\right)_{ij}-\left(\mathcal{C}_{n}^{(i)}\right)_{ij}\right|.

Applying (
A.11
) and the assumption in (
A.7
), we obtain

(A.15)

\left(\mathcal{C}_{n}E_{a}\mathcal{C}_{n}^{\dagger}\right)_{ii}-\left(\mathcal{C}_{n}^{(i)}E_{a}\left(\mathcal{C}_{n}^{(i)}\right)^{\dagger}\right)_{ii}\prec(W\ell\eta)^{-2n+1}\cdot\left(\Xi_{2n}^{(\mathrm{d})}+1\right)^{1/2}.

To conclude (
A.14
), it remains to estimate
\mathcal{C}_{n}^{(i)}E_{a}\left(\mathcal{C}_{n}^{(i)}\right)^{\dagger}
. Recall the definition of
\mathcal{C}_{n}^{(ii)}
in (
A.10
). Using (
4.8
), we write:

\left(\mathcal{C}_{n}^{(i)}E_{a}\left(\mathcal{C}_{n}^{(i)}\right)^{\dagger}\right)_{ii}=|G_{ii}|^{2}\cdot\left(H\cdot\mathcal{C}_{n}^{(ii)}E_{a}\left(\mathcal{C}_{n}^{(ii)}\right)^{\dagger}\cdot H\right)_{ii}.

Since
\mathcal{C}_{n}^{(ii)}
is independent of
\{H_{ik}\}_{k=1}^{N}
, we apply Lemma 3.3 from
[
23
]
to obtain

(A.16)

\left(\mathcal{C}_{n}^{(i)}E_{a}\left(\mathcal{C}_{n}^{(i)}\right)^{\dagger}\right)_{ii}=|G_{ii}|^{2}\sum_{k}S_{ik}\left(\mathcal{C}_{n}^{(ii)}E_{a}\left(\mathcal{C}_{n}^{(ii)}\right)^{\dagger}\right)_{kk}+O_{\prec}\left(\sum_{kl}S_{ik}\left|\left(\mathcal{C}_{n}^{(ii)}E_{a}\left(\mathcal{C}_{n}^{(ii)}\right)^{\dagger}\right)_{kl}\right|^{2}S_{li}\right)^{1/2}.

The first term on the right can be bounded as
2n
-chain loops with
G^{(i)}
entries, while the second can be interpreted as
4n
-chain loops with
G^{(i)}
entries. Similar to (
5.118
), we can bound
4n-G^{(i)}
loop with two
2n-G^{(i)}
loops as follows

\Xi_{4n}^{\mathcal{L},(i)}\prec\left(\Xi_{2n}^{\mathcal{L},(i)}\right)^{2}\cdot(W\ell\eta).

Using (
A.12
), we have

\Xi_{2n}^{\mathcal{L},(i)}\prec\left(\Xi_{2n}^{(\mathrm{d})}+(W\ell\eta)^{1/2}\right)\cdot(W\ell\eta)^{-2n+1/2}.

Hence,

\left(\mathcal{C}_{n}^{(i)}E_{a}\left(\mathcal{C}_{n}^{(i)}\right)^{\dagger}\right)_{ii}\prec\left(\Xi_{2n}^{(\mathrm{d})}+(W\ell\eta)^{1/2}\right)\cdot(W\ell\eta)^{-2n+1/2}.

Combining this with (
A.15
), we conclude

\left(\mathcal{C}_{n}E_{a}\mathcal{C}_{n}^{\dagger}\right)_{ii}\prec(W\ell\eta)^{-2n+1}\cdot\left(\left(\Xi_{2n}^{(\mathrm{d})}\right)^{1/2}+\Xi_{2n}^{(\mathrm{d})}\cdot(W\ell\eta)^{-1/2}+1\right),

which establishes (
A.14
).

We now prove

(A.17)

\Xi^{(\mathrm{o})}_{n}\prec 1,

using (
A.11
) and (
A.13
). By (
4.8
), we express
\left(\mathcal{C}_{n}^{(i)}\right)_{ij}
in terms of
\mathcal{C}_{n}^{(ii)}
as

(A.18)

\left(\mathcal{C}_{n}^{(i)}\right)_{ij}=(G_{1})_{ii}\cdot\left(H\cdot\mathcal{C}_{n}^{(ii)}\right)_{ij}.

Since
\mathcal{C}_{n}^{(ii)}
is independent of
\{H_{ik}\}_{k=1}^{N}
, we again apply Lemma 3.3 of
[
23
]
:

(A.19)

\displaystyle\left(H\cdot\mathcal{C}_{n}^{(ii)}\right)_{ij}

\displaystyle\prec\left(\sum_{k}S_{ik}\left|\left(\mathcal{C}_{n}^{(ii)}\right)_{kj}\right|^{2}\right)^{1/2}\prec\sum_{a}\mathbf{1}(|a-[i]|\leq 1)\cdot\left(\mathcal{C}_{n}^{(ii)}E_{a}\mathcal{C}_{n}^{(ii)\dagger}\right)_{jj}^{1/2}.

Combining this with (
A.18
), we find that for
i\neq j
,

(A.20)

\left(\mathcal{C}_{n}^{(i)}\right)_{ij}\prec\max_{a}\left(\mathcal{C}_{n}^{(ii)}E_{a}\mathcal{C}_{n}^{(ii)\dagger}\right)_{jj}^{1/2}.

Using this estimate along with (
A.13
) and (
A.11
), we obtain

\left(\mathcal{C}_{n}\right)_{ij}\prec(W\ell\eta)^{-n+1/2}\left(1+\Xi_{n}^{(\mathrm{o})}\cdot(W\ell\eta)^{-1/4}\right),

which yields the desired bound (
A.17
).

Proof of (
A.11
).
Using (
4.9
) to express
G^{(i)}
in (
A.9
) in terms of
G
, and bounding
1/G_{ii}=O(1)
via Theorem
2.3
, we can estimate the difference
\left(\mathcal{C}_{n}\right)_{ij}-\left(\mathcal{C}^{(i)}_{n}\right)_{ij}
in terms of products of diagonal and off-diagonal chain entries. For example, for
n=2
, we have:

(A.21)

\displaystyle\left(\mathcal{C}^{(i)}_{2}\right)_{ij}-\left(\mathcal{C}_{2}\right)_{ij}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\left(G_{1}E_{a_{1}}G_{2}\right)_{ii}\cdot(G_{2})_{ij}}{(G_{2})_{ii}}=O\left(\Xi^{(\mathrm{d})}_{2}\cdot\Xi^{(\mathrm{o})}_{1}\cdot(W\ell\eta)^{-3/2}\right).

For
n=3
, we similarly write:

(A.22)

\displaystyle\left(\mathcal{C}^{(i)}_{3}\right)_{ij}-\left(\mathcal{C}_{3}\right)_{ij}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\left(G_{1}E_{a_{1}}G_{2}\right)_{ii}\cdot\left(G_{2}E_{a_{2}}G_{3}\right)_{ij}}{(G_{2})_{ii}}-\frac{\left(G_{1}E_{a_{1}}G_{2}E_{a_{2}}G_{3}\right)_{ii}\cdot(G_{3})_{ij}}{(G_{3})_{ii}}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\left(G_{1}E_{a_{1}}G_{2}\right)_{ii}\cdot\left(G_{2}E_{a_{2}}G_{3}\right)_{ii}\cdot(G_{3})_{ij}}{(G_{2})_{ii}(G_{3})_{ii}}

(A.23)

\displaystyle=O\left(\Xi^{(\mathrm{d})}_{2}\Xi^{(\mathrm{o})}_{2}+\Xi^{(\mathrm{d})}_{3}\Xi^{(\mathrm{o})}_{1}+\Xi^{(\mathrm{d})}_{2}\Xi^{(\mathrm{d})}_{2}\Xi^{(\mathrm{o})}_{1}\right)\cdot(W\ell\eta)^{-5/2}.

In general, we obtain the bound:

(A.24)

\displaystyle\left(\mathcal{C}_{n}\right)_{ij}-\left(\mathcal{C}^{(i)}_{n}\right)_{ij}\prec\sum_{k\geq 1}\sum_{\{n_{i}\}_{i=1}^{k}}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{k}\Xi^{(\mathrm{d})}_{n_{i}}\right)\Xi^{(\mathrm{o})}_{\ell}\cdot(W\ell\eta)^{-n+1/2}\cdot\mathbf{1}\left(\ell+\sum_{i=1}^{k}n_{i}=n+k\right),

where
2\leq n_{i}\leq n\leq 2n-2
, and
1\leq\ell\leq n-1
. Applying the induction hypothesis (
A.7
) to all shorter chains involved, we get:

\Xi^{(\mathrm{d})}_{n_{i}}+\Xi^{(\mathrm{o})}_{\ell}\prec 1,

which implies (
A.11
).

Proof of (
A.12
).
Observe that
\left(\mathcal{C}^{(ii)}_{n}E_{a}\mathcal{C}^{(ii)\dagger}_{n}\right)_{jj}
is a diagonal entry of a
2n
-
G^{(i)}
chain, while
\left(\mathcal{C}_{n}E_{a}\mathcal{C}^{\dagger}_{n}\right)_{jj}
is the same object but with
G^{(i)}
replaced by
G
. Applying (
4.9
) to express all
G^{(i)}
in terms of
G
, and again bounding
1/G_{ii}=O(1)
, we estimate the difference:

\left\langle\mathcal{C}^{(ii)}_{n}E_{a}\mathcal{C}^{(ii)\dagger}_{n}E_{b}\right\rangle-\left\langle\mathcal{C}_{n}E_{a}\mathcal{C}^{\dagger}_{n}E_{b}\right\rangle.

This difference involves products of diagonal chains, and similar to (
A.24
), we obtain:

(A.25)

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathcal{C}^{(ii)}_{n}E_{a}\mathcal{C}^{(ii)\dagger}_{n}E_{b}\right\rangle-\left\langle\mathcal{C}_{n}E_{a}\mathcal{C}^{\dagger}_{n}E_{b}\right\rangle\prec\sum_{k\geq 1}\sum_{\{n_{i}\}_{i=1}^{k}}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{k}\Xi^{(\mathrm{d})}_{n_{i}}\right)\cdot(W\ell\eta)^{-2n}\cdot\mathbf{1}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{k}n_{i}=2n+k\right),

with
2\leq n_{i}\leq 2n+1
.
By the inductive assumption (
A.7
), we can bound:

\left(\prod_{i=1}^{k}\Xi^{(\mathrm{d})}_{n_{i}}\right)\cdot\mathbf{1}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{k}n_{i}=2n+k\right)\;\;\prec\;\;1+\mathbf{1}_{n=2}\left(\Xi^{(\mathrm{d})}_{2n-1}\right)^{2}+\Xi^{(\mathrm{d})}_{2n}+\Xi^{(\mathrm{d})}_{2n+1}.

Applying the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality yields:

\Xi^{(\mathrm{d})}_{2n-1}\prec\left(\Xi^{(\mathrm{d})}_{2n-2}\cdot\Xi^{(\mathrm{d})}_{2n}\right)^{1/2}\prec\left(\Xi^{(\mathrm{d})}_{2n}\right)^{1/2}.

Furthermore, splitting a
2n+1
-chain into two
n
-chains and one 1-chain, and using a triple-product Cauchy–Schwarz inequality (i.e.,
\int f(x)g(y)h(x,y)\leq\|f\|_{2}\|g\|_{2}\|h\|_{2}
), we get:

\Xi^{(\mathrm{d})}_{2n+1}\prec\Xi^{(\mathrm{d})}_{2n}\cdot(W\ell\eta)^{1/2}.

Putting all of these bounds into (
A.25
), we conclude the proof of (
A.12
).

Proof of (
A.13
).
Similar to the expansion in (
A.25
), we estimate the difference:

(A.26)

\displaystyle\left(\mathcal{C}^{(ii)}_{n}E_{a}\mathcal{C}^{(ii)\,\dagger}_{n}\right)_{jj}-\left(\mathcal{C}_{n}E_{a}\mathcal{C}^{\dagger}_{n}\right)_{jj}

\displaystyle\;\prec\;

\displaystyle\sum_{k\geq 0}\sum_{\{n_{i}\}_{i=1}^{k}}\sum_{l_{1},\,l_{2}}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{k}\Xi^{(\mathrm{d})}_{n_{i}}\right)\left(\prod_{j=1}^{2}\Xi^{(\mathrm{o})}_{l_{j}}\right)\cdot(W\ell\eta)^{-2n}\cdot\mathbf{1}\left(l_{1}+l_{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{k}n_{i}=2n+k+1\right),

where the index ranges satisfy:

2\leq n_{i}\leq 2n,\qquad 1\leq l_{1}\leq l_{2}\leq 2n.

Under the assumptions on
\Xi^{(\mathrm{d})}_{n_{i}}
, it suffices to bound the product of off-diagonal terms. That is, we only need to show that:

(A.27)

\displaystyle l_{1}+l_{2}\leq 2n+1\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\Xi^{(\mathrm{o})}_{l_{1}}\cdot\Xi^{(\mathrm{o})}_{l_{2}}\prec\left(\left(\Xi^{(\mathrm{o})}_{n}\right)^{2}+1\right)\cdot(W\ell\eta)^{1/2}.

By the inductive assumption, we have

\Xi^{(\mathrm{o})}_{l_{j}}\prec\begin{cases}1,&l_{j}\leq n-1,\\[6.0pt]
\Xi^{(\mathrm{o})}_{n},&l_{j}=n,\\[6.0pt]
(W\ell\eta)^{1/2},&n<l_{j}\leq 2n,\end{cases}

where for
l_{j}>n
, the bound follows from applying the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality.
These bounds clearly imply (
A.27
), and hence we obtain (
A.13
). This completes the proof of Lemma
A.2
.

∎
